--- Page 43 ---
<meta>This page continues the critique of "false cures" for the soul. The previous page ended by discussing how penance without true love of God is a shaky foundation. This page lists specific internal sins and critiques those who judge their spiritual state only by outward behavior.</meta>

Such as sloth, suspicion, ambition, envy, hatred, revenge, impatience, **carnal security** <note>A theological term for a false sense of safety or overconfidence in one's spiritual state while still living in sin.</note>, spiritual pride, presumption, wicked judgment, tyranny, unbelief, a delusion of holiness above others, trust in oneself and one's own merit, as well as the contempt of Christ, the fabrication of new riches of heavenly revelation, and whatever other such plagues may be found among us spiritually sick people. 

Often, when we are at peace, we do not feel these things for a time—or we regard them but little—due to a great, imagined holiness. But in the end, when the **gnawing worm** <note>A common historical metaphor for the conscience or the persistent sting of guilt and regret.</note>, which had lain still through the cunning of Satan, begins to stir again—when God's judgment upon all flesh breaks forth in earnest—then it is soon discovered, to our harm, that apart from Christ, the eternally life-giving Physician of our souls, no true healing before God and no good conscience can ever stand or exist for all eternity.

There are also other errant people to whom the sickness of their souls remains so entirely unknown (perhaps through a lack of teaching) that they cannot even initially consider themselves sinners in any earnest way. This is because they judge or view the nature of sin and sickness only according to coarse, outward vices and not according to the evil desires and the wounding of the human heart. Therefore, they believe that as long as their desires do not break out into deeds, they need have no repentance or sorrow over them. 

Such people do not wish to grant Jesus Christ His honor by letting Him remain their **Throne of Grace** <note>Refers to the mercy seat in the Tabernacle; in Christian theology, it signifies that God’s grace is accessible through Christ.</note>, High Priest, and heavenly Physician. Though they may often name, confess, and even call upon Him, it does not happen with the intention that He should heal their soul's sickness or forgive their sins. Rather, they do it only so that through Him and the intercession of all the saints, they might have a higher rank than other people and find full satisfaction in heaven. Thus, they wish to accumulate many merits for the delight of their flesh and their puffed-up minds, and to "buy" heaven from God with their own devised works.

The intentions, orders, and regulations of the aforementioned people have often been directed entirely toward this goal. Just like children, they have little more than their own fabricated, fleshly thoughts concerning God, Christ, heaven, and eternal blessedness. This all stems from the fact that they do not recognize themselves or their soul's sickness at the outset. Consequently, they do not seek Christ as the only heavenly Physician with His medicine from the bottom of their hearts, nor do they desire or perceive His healing. However, how false and unjust the opinion of these people is before God—and in what a dangerous state such "healthy-sick" people are, even despite a strict and difficult life—can easily be calculated and found from what has been said previously.

It critiques those who judge their holiness by outward actions alone, accusing them of trying to "buy" heaven through self-devised merits.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of spiritual self-examination. It transitions from listing internal sins to the necessity of finding a "Physician" (Christ) rather than relying on outward religious rituals or a literalist understanding of scripture.</meta>

-> ~~~~~ <-

I have wanted to show these points here as an example, so that we all first learn to recognize—completely and thoroughly—our plague and sickness in all the corners, qualities, and schemes of the **carnal heart** <note>In this context, the "carnal heart" refers to human nature driven by worldly desires rather than spiritual devotion.</note>, which is sin with all its power, both inward and outward. For we are all (by nature) like the unclean, and all our righteousness is like a filthy garment. We have all withered like leaves, and our sins carry us away like a wind. Isaiah 64:6.

Secondly, it is not enough to simply recognize our sin and sickness; rather, we must also learn to know the Physician, to believe in him, and to love, praise, and seek him. Only then does the comfort of the Holy Spirit truly begin—as it did with the Prophet David—through which we become entirely assured and certain of a joyful recovery. What does he say of such things? Let all who trust in him rejoice: they shall boast eternally, for he desires to dwell with them; (it is a glorious thing when one has a good, well-proven physician always with him in the house); let all who love his name be joyful in him. Psalm 5:12.

Otherwise, it often happens in our times that there are some who recognize their sickness only superficially. Unlike the first group, they do not try to treat it with good works, yet they only understand their illness through the "letter" <note>The "letter" refers to a literal or intellectual reading of the Bible without a corresponding internal spiritual experience.</note>. Thus, they also wish to heal it superficially with writings and signs or other outward things and customs. Their conscience, faith, and health are built solely on the promise of the "letter," or on certain verses of Scripture without the Spirit, as well as without any improvement of their lives. Indeed, when they hear and do not act, they build only on **dead sand** <note>A reference to the parable in Matthew 7:26-27 about the foolish man who builds his house on sand.</note>, and they refuse to go further to the Rock, Christ, in whom all promises are fulfilled. He alone distributes all heavenly goods; indeed, he alone gives salvation, grace, and eternal health. Holy Scripture and all outward church customs point toward him, just as they gloriously boast, teach, and testify of him, of his office, and of his honor (which he gives to no other).

To this heavenly Physician, Christ himself, one must come in the obedience of faith. One must lift the heart above all creatures, above all human laws, signs, orders, rules, or works, and direct it into heaven, if one wishes to soon become whole, blessed, and healthy. For he will deliver the poor who cry out, and the wretched who have no helper: he will be gracious to the lowly and the poor, and he will help the souls of the needy. Psalm 72:13.

# The 11th Chapter.

### Concerning the State of Unrepentant Worldlings in Regard to Spiritual Sickness and Medicine.

But what shall we say of the wicked worldlings, in whom there is no fear of God, who in no way heed or perceive either their sickness, the Physician’s medicine, or spiritual health? For their heart is so possessed by temporal honor <note>“Temporal honor” refers to the fleeting status and reputation granted by society, rather than eternal standing with God.</note>, wealth, pride, and the lust of the flesh, that they have their kingdom of heaven therein and wish to inquire after no other—indeed, not even after GOD—with any earnestness. Of these, David speaks in the Psalm, where he calls them fools, saying: The fools say in their heart, "There is no GOD"; they are corrupt and do abominable deeds; there is none who does good. Psalm 14:1. Even though such people are thoroughly corrupted to the very core with deadly poison in their soul and conscience, and are a foul, stinking carcass in the eyes of God, yet, alas, they do not feel it. They consider everything of little account, go about in full joy, and behave as if they were entirely healthy and were to live and remain here forever. Oh, how gladly they would leave heaven to GOD if they could only keep the world and remain sitting in it forever in peace—which is often clearly shown by their gestures when they are about to die. But it shall not be so, you men, for GOD is Lord not only of heaven, but also of the earth. Psalm 24:1. Ah, how utterly nothing are all men who live so securely on earth, Selah. Psalm 39:5 <note>The original German cites Psalm 39:6, following the numbering found in many older German Bibles.</note>.

Such worldly people, who do not inquire after GOD in earnest, might not unfairly be compared in their conduct to a bound madman who, though very sick and destroyed and shattered in all his reason, yet cries out, sings, and laughs without ceasing, as if he were entirely healthy, free, and unburdened. Therefore, he will not listen to any physician, much less acknowledge his sickness, nor take or use any of the medicine, tearing open his bandaged wounds <term>etc.</term> <note>original: "2c." — an abbreviation for "et cetera."</note>. Just so are they—the wicked, ungodly children of the world—before the angels of God: they are shackled and bound, full of sin and filth. When one speaks to them out of love and faithfulness concerning their inner sickness and the Physician, Christ—about true healing and the Kingdom of God—they mock and despise it all, preferring to perish rather than be made whole; indeed, they may well say: "I do not concern myself with that thing" (for so they call the...

<meta>The text continues the medical metaphor from the previous page, shifting from the general state of the "unrepentant" to their specific refusal to hear the "Gospel of Christ." It uses the catchword "ihr" (you/their) to link the previous page to the current discussion of those who avoid the Physician.</meta>

(knowledge of the Gospel of Christ and its recognition) means nothing to me; I do not wish to hear it, it only makes me go astray, etc. I must care for my children, I cannot wait upon him <note>The "him" refers to Christ as the Great Physician.</note>, and I should also do other things that God has commanded.

Behold, is this not a certain sign of the deadly sickness of their poor souls? Is it not a true plague and an indication of internal ulcers, poisonous boils <note>original: "giftiger Blattern" - likely referring to smallpox or similar pustules</note>, and their unhealthy spiritual stomach and conscience? For just as a healthy, robust person may taste all kinds of physical food, all kinds of drink, spice, or necessity with measure and deliberation, to choose from them what might be most suitable for his constitution <term>Complexion: A term from humoral medicine referring to a person’s physical and mental temperament.</term> or nature: so, indeed, may one who is robust or healthy in his soul, spirit, and conscience also try all kinds of teaching, all kinds of spiritual food and internal nourishment, provided it happens with measure—that is, with a spiritual, ruminating judgment <note>The author uses the metaphor of "ruminating" (chewing the cud) to describe the slow, meditative process of spiritual discernment.</note> in the fear of God. To this, St. Paul has exhorted all spiritual, healthy—that is, true Christian—people when he says: *Do not quench the Spirit, do not despise prophecy, but test all things*—that is, try everything—*and hold fast to what is good.* Likewise, the Lord Christ himself says: *He who has ears to hear, let him hear,* and after that: *Take heed what you hear.* Mark 4:23. And again to his disciples: *Are you also still without understanding?* Matthew 15:16.

Therefore, the aforementioned people, who shy away from the true knowledge of the heavenly Physician Jesus Christ and from the healing teaching of his eternal Gospel—as if they would be led astray in their conscience by it—show by this very thing that they have never yet become right, nor whole, nor understanding in their soul and conscience. Indeed, they have never yet earnestly desired, sought, or considered the way of righteousness, the light of life, and the health of their souls. For any man who feels an untried disgust for every foreign food (which actually brings more benefit than harm) must certainly not have a good stomach. Likewise, it is for many nowadays: when one tells them of the right foundation of the medicine of Christ, of repentance <note>original: "Buße" - can mean both repentance and penance.</note>, of his soul-saving knowledge and heavenly kingdom, they do not like to hear it; they despise it, it seems too high for them, and their "old Adam" <note>The "old Adam" refers to the fallen, sinful nature of humanity that resists spiritual transformation.</note> shrinks from it in horror. For they fear that their bad stomach, their internal sickness, and the filth of their evil conscience will be stirred and shaken up by it.

If one then also tells them, out of faithful warning, of future harm—such as the punishment of sins, the earnest judgment of GOD, etc.—they feel sick at heart and they recoil against it, just as one pours wormwood juice <note>Wormwood is famously bitter and was used both as a medicine and a metaphor for sorrow or divine punishment.</note> into a delicate child; their flesh would rather have something soft, something merry, something lovely—

<summary>This page uses the metaphor of a "bad stomach" to explain why people reject the Gospel, comparing spiritual discernment to the ability of a healthy person to digest different foods. It argues that avoiding the "bitter medicine" of repentance and divine judgment is a symptom of a soul that has never been truly healed.</summary>

# The 12th Chapter.

### How the world cannot bear for the Physician to reveal its deadly sickness, much less for him to help it.

It is a great pity that the world not only refuses to be helped, but its people also do not want to hear a word about their great damage. Isaiah prophesied about this when God commanded him to use the Jewish people as a figure <note>In this context, a "figure" acts as a symbolic representation or archetype for all of humanity.</note> to proclaim the sickness of the entire world, saying: *Go now and write it before them on a tablet, and record it in a book, that it may remain forever and ever; for it is a rebellious people and lying children who do not want to hear the law of the Lord* (meaning, the law that leads to true health), *but say to the seers (or prophets): "You shall not see"* (which means to stifle the Spirit of God), *and to the visionaries: "You shall not envision the right doctrine for us"* (that is, you should not terrify us at all or uncover our hidden sickness), *“but preach softly to us”* (of health; speak what pleases us and gladdens our heart), *“show us deceptions”* (that is, the hypocrisy of the flesh), *“turn aside from this way”* (the way of terrifying the conscience), *“depart from this path; let the Holy One of Israel cease from before us,”* Isaiah 30:8–11.

As if they were to say: We do not want you to tell us so much about spiritual unrest, plague, and sickness; let the heavenly Physician stay in heaven and spend his bitter medicine elsewhere. # Chapter 13.

### How the soul’s sickness may be rightly recognized, repented of, and improved.

Therefore, it is necessary that all people, whatever they may be called, first rightly recognize the great evil of their inner sickness here below, if they wish to be fit for the Kingdom of God and be made whole and healthy in their souls by the true Physician, Jesus Christ. Such recognition happens primarily through the suffering of Jesus Christ, the Son of God, when the Holy Spirit applies it to a person through the love of God. For it must surely be no small plague, no minor sickness, debt, or damage, for which the eternal God gave His only-begotten Son into torment, suffering, and shameful death. As Augustine <note>St. Augustine of Hippo (354–430 AD), a foundational theologian whose writings often emphasized human frailty and the need for divine grace.</note> says: the precious cost of the medicine proves how severe the sickness is.

Now, Christ was given over to death for the sake of our sins—that is, for the sake of the sickness of our souls and the corruption of all people—as Paul says in Rom. 4:25. "For the sake of the sins of my people," says God the Father, "I have struck him," Isa. 53:4-5. And He, the Son Himself, says: "I must pay for what I did not steal," Ps. 69:5. <note>In the 17th-century context, this Psalm was often interpreted as Christ taking on a debt (sin) that He did not personally commit.</note> If the most beloved and righteous Child was thus struck and not spared, how will...

Therefore, even if a person were so utterly without understanding, so cold and coarse, that they could not easily come to a realization of their soul’s misery (that is, of themselves and their sins), they should at least look somewhat more closely at their own heart, works, thoughts, nature, and conduct. A person should first reflect on all of this regarding the suffering of Christ: that they may truly perceive the earnest wrath of God toward unrepentant sinners who do not, through faith, partake in the suffering and merit of Christ; and that they may ask God for grace in repentance and sorrow. For if a person does not come to self-knowledge through the suffering of Christ and the word of the cross—gaining repentance and sorrow for their sin, and indeed a true displeasure with their entire **old Adam** <note>A theological term referring to the fallen, sinful nature of humanity inherited from Adam, which must be "put off" to live a spiritual life.</note>—then the suffering of Christ has not yet been truly useful to them.

When they have then come from the heart to a realization of their sinful guilt and now desire the Physician’s help and counsel—that is, to be delivered from those same sins and to become pious, healthy, and blessed—they should shortly thereafter also grasp and take to heart the great, inexpressible love, goodness, and mercy of God toward all Christian believers through the suffering of Christ. Both of these points are lovely and sequentially shown to us by the Lord Christ in the book of Isaiah, where He speaks thus to the poor, sick sinner: *You have burdened me with your sins and wearied me with your iniquities;* (this is the first part, and the second follows:) *I, even I* (that is, Christ) *blot out your transgressions for my own sake, and remember your sins no more.* <margin>Isaiah 43:24–25.</margin> O, what a lovely word of merciful promise from God for us poor, sick human beings!

# Chapter 14.

### Regarding the Use and Fruit of the Suffering, Death, and Resurrection of the Physician Jesus Christ.

For Christ was not only given over to death for the sake of our sin and sickness, but he was also raised again for the sake of our righteousness, so that through him we might be made righteous and become heirs of eternal life. In his suffering, he took our sin and sickness upon himself, paid the full price for them through his death, released us from them, and put them to death. But through his resurrection (in which he became our righteousness), he shows us how we—through faith in his life—become righteous, healthy, whole, and blessed. Just as there is now no wound, no pain, nor any sickness in the resurrected, reigning Christ, so too can he heal us before God in an instant when we are engrafted <note>A metaphor for being spiritually joined to Christ, like a branch grafted onto a vine.</note> into him and accept this Lord and heavenly Physician through faith, as the one who died for our sin and rose for our justification. -> ❧❧❧❧❧ <-

He lived among the heathens in idolatry and all manner of injustice, and this took the upper hand in him as well as among the people; but when the Lord allowed him to be led captive to Babylon, to fall into sorrow, misery, and anguish, only then did he recognize what he could not see during his time of prosperity and peace because of pride, worldly pleasure, and temporal splendor. He recognized the sin and the hidden sickness of his soul; he pleaded, begged, and humbled himself before God the Lord. Indeed, the Prayer of Manasseh <note>The Prayer of Manasseh is a short apocryphal text traditionally attributed to the King of Judah after his capture by the Assyrians/Babylonians, focusing on deep repentance.</note> shows this most beautifully: Then God heard him and brought him back to Jerusalem to his kingdom. Then Manasseh recognized (for the first time truly) that God is the Lord, 2 Chronicles 33:13. As it is also written elsewhere regarding the children of Israel: When he slew them, they sought him, and they returned and enquired early after God; and they remembered that God was their refuge, and the High God their redeemer. Psalm 78:34-35.

What else is the Parable of the Prodigal Son in Luke 15? While he enjoyed his father’s grace and favor—indeed, while he had all his courage and goods—he in no way wished to recognize himself; he did not want to perceive or consider the sickness of his soul at all. But when he fell into misery, hunger, and grief, he came to his senses <note>original: "schlug er in sich selbst," literally "he struck within himself," an idiom for a sudden, deep realization or conscience-stricken moment.</note>, recognized the evil that was in him, and said: "O Father, I have sinned against heaven and before you, I am no longer worthy to be called your son," <term>etc.</term> <note>original: "2c." – an abbreviation for *et cetera*.</note> See, thus God must also often discipline us for our own benefit and to prevent eternal harm, perhaps to make us wise and sensible; He must put a bit and bridle into our mouths, like horses and mules, if we will not turn to Him in love; if we neither heed the sickness of our souls nor earnestly perceive the health that is in Christ. "O, it is good for me, Lord, that you have (also) humbled me, that I may learn your statutes; for before I was humbled, I went astray; but now I keep your word. This is my comfort in my misery, for your word refreshes me," says King David, and with him all those who stand in repentance and under the cross of Christ, Psalm 119:67, 71.

# Chapter 15.

### How the Entire Sum of Salvation and the Spiritual Health of Man Consists in Twofold Knowledge: Namely, of Christ the Physician and of Oneself.

Let this now be said concerning the sickness of the soul, the burden of evil, and its recognition, and where we should comfortingly seek refreshment and health. For in these two parts lies the entire foundation of your **salvation**: first, that you thoroughly recognize your sickness (that is, sin, yourself, and what you are by nature—namely, a corrupted person, dead to God in body and soul); and second, that you rightly learn to consider the Physician and Savior (that is, Christ) and what you are to become through Him. One without the other is fundamentally nothing before God, just as the fear of God without the love of God, and just as faith without works also cannot be anything. We have already heard this in part in the aforementioned examples <note>Referring to King Manasseh and the Prodigal Son from the previous page.</note> and, God willing, shall hear it further. Of this the Prophet David also speaks in the Psalms, where he says: *They who sow in tears* (that is, nailing the old sinful, sick man to the Cross with Christ in order to become healthy) *shall reap with joy* (a new, healthy person), *and carry noble seed* (the Word of God) *and come with joy* (to Christ the Physician) *and bring their sheaves.* <note>A paraphrased interpretation of Psalm 126:5–6.</note> For what else is man by nature but a sick person who is to become healthy through Christ?

Whoever truly finds in themselves such recognition of their innate inner sickness with repentance and sorrow, and wishes to be free of it and turns back, is already somewhat enlightened by the light of <term>prevenient grace</term> <note>original: "vorkommenden Gnade." A theological term for grace that "comes before" or precedes human decision, moving a person toward God.</note>; he has the beginning of <term>rebirth</term> for the commencement of his recovery in his heart. For by nature, man cannot have this recognition rightly; indeed, health has already blessedly begun in such a person through the preceding sanctifying grace. The more divine light he has within him and the more he conforms himself to the work of God, the more he increases and grows before God in these two parts. Therefore, in the Christian faith, it is a very necessary thing to have a true knowledge of oneself and one's innate sickness; without this, one can in no way desire, love, nor accept and recognize the heavenly Physician Jesus Christ, even if He is present. But as already said, it is not enough that you recognize yourself and your sickness and that you sow with tears, but

It describes the painful process of repentance as "sowing in tears" which leads to the joy of spiritual rebirth through grace.</summary>

In Luke 1:77, this is called a "knowledge of salvation," for which John the Baptist was to prepare the way; it is explained shortly thereafter by Zechariah as consisting in the forgiveness of sins through the tender mercy of God, by which the **rising sun from on high** <note>original: "Aufgang aus der Höhe." This is a traditional reference to the "Dayspring" or "Dawn" in the Song of Zechariah (Luke 1:78).</note>—that is, Christ the heavenly Physician—has visited us.

In Holy Scripture, this is also called the art or knowledge of the saints of God. Where such knowledge is lacking, the soul is not well; see the Book of Wisdom 13:10–19 <note>The author cites "Sap. 13," referring to the *Sapientia Salomonis* or Wisdom of Solomon, an apococryphal book often included in German Bibles of this era.</note> regarding how vain and indulgent all people are who do not have the knowledge of God. The Lord says in Hosea 6:6: *For I desire steadfast love and not sacrifice, the knowledge of God rather than burnt offerings.* Now, if anyone chooses to remain ignorant of all this, let them be ignorant; as Paul says, God will also not want to know or recognize them on that [Last] Day. And again, in 1 Corinthians 14:20: *Dear brothers, do not be children in your understanding; rather, be children in malice, but in understanding be perfect.* Of the Pharisees, Christ says that they are blind and leaders of the blind, because they never truly recognized themselves, nor Christ, nor God His Father (Matthew 15:14; John 8:55).

# Chapter 16.

### That the Heavenly Physician, Christ, Heals All Injuries and Sicknesses, Save for One.

At this point, perhaps something more should be distinguished concerning the various properties of the aforementioned spiritual sickness—that is, the various kinds of sin. However, this is rightly known only to the Physician, who knows and recognizes every kind of sickness, including its root and how manifold the damage is. But I know, praise God, that no sin, no damage, nor infirmity of the soul is so great, or could ever be so great, that this Physician (who has promised to give His help for free) could not heal it, release us from it, and make us healthy—excepting only one damage, which is called the **sin against the Holy Spirit**. <term>sin against the Holy Spirit: Historically interpreted here as a conscious, heart-hardened rejection of Christ's divinity and His power to forgive.</term> This occurs when one despises and slanders this Physician, Jesus Christ, and refuses to accept Him from the heart; when one refuses to recognize Him as the true, natural Son of God, or to believe that the Holy Spirit is in Him and through Him—indeed, that He Himself in the Holy Spirit forgives sin—and that He is the true Savior, Redeemer, and Healer of our sick souls. If a person once sees all this in the light...

<summary>This page concludes Chapter 15's argument that spiritual health requires knowing Christ's mercy and begins Chapter 16, asserting that Christ can heal any sin except the deliberate rejection of His divinity, known as the sin against the Holy Spirit.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the theological argument that while most sins can be healed by the "Heavenly Physician," the deliberate rejection of the Holy Spirit’s work—as seen in the Pharisees—places a person beyond help. It then transitions into an encouraging description of Christ’s "pharmacy" of grace, which is more accessible in the current age than ever before.</meta>

...recognized [the knowledge] of grace, and yet denies it or disregards it again—one who also calls Christ's works, which He performs in the Holy Spirit, the work of the Devil, indeed calling the Holy Spirit a devil, as the Pharisees and scribes did out of envy and a malicious heart—for such a person, it is impossible that their soul be helped either in this world or the next; rather, they are guilty of eternal judgment. Matthew 12:31.

We poor, miserable, sick human beings have thus described our lovely Lord and blessed Physician, Jesus Christ (from whom no sick person who comes to Him and stays with Him can fall away), in His office of medicine and healing. We have shown that He has made satisfaction not just for one sin and sickness, not just for **original sin**, but for all sins—indeed, for the sins of the whole world—and knows how to find help. He does not heal like other physical physicians with mere patchwork, fixing only some injuries until death; rather, He heals unto true health and eternal life. He brings the soul into the immortal divine essence and heals even all old injuries (where often there was no hope for recovery). However, He heals these things only if one is willing to recognize, love, and accept Him as the Physician. He heals the deadly wounds of our heart and conscience so finely, smoothly, and correctly that not a single scar or blemish remains before the eyes of God, so that we may become entirely assured and certain of our health through faith.

Therefore, the healing of deathly ill, sinful souls is still today the office of the reigning King, Christ—an office and business of His heavenly kingdom. It occurs within the government of grace. His <term>pharmacy</term> <note>original: "Apotheke." In the 17th century, this was a common metaphor for the Gospel or the Church, where spiritual "medicines" (grace and the Word) were dispensed to the suffering soul.</note> stands open at all times; He gives everything for free out of grace. This is where the invitations in the Prophets and Psalms belong, calling us all to come to Him so that our souls may become whole and healthy. Isaiah 55:1–2; Psalm 95:6–7. No sick person can excuse themselves by claiming it is the Physician’s fault if they do not recover.

Furthermore, this pharmacy is opened far, far wider in our times than it was many hundreds of years ago; Christ graciously allows Himself to be rightly recognized again, showing that soul-health is to be found in Him alone. Therefore, let no sick person delay, but today—while it is still "today"—run and race to this Physician, and seek the Lord while He is to be found. We should seek the Lord and Physician Jesus Christ Himself with love and faith, and not run elsewhere for spiritual medicine to heal our soul's sickness, as the Jews did, whom the Lord rebukes in Hosea 5:13, saying: *When Ephraim saw his sickness, and Judah his wounds, then Ephraim went to Assyria to King Jareb; but he could not heal you or [cure] your wounds...*

This page emphasizes that Christ's healing leaves no scars and is more accessible to the faithful now than in previous centuries.</summary>

One should only come, believe in him, accept him, hope in him, and allow the medicine for eternal health to be applied to oneself; for the medicine and the physician are ready as often as one comes, whether by day or night, for everyone who needs it. It lasts eternally, does not diminish, preserves, heals, and makes all the sick healthy who use it for eternal life forever, Amen.

# Chapter 17.

### Concerning Three Kinds of Sick People.

With those who are physically ill, things happen in three ways: Some immediately seek the best physician and follow his advice as soon as they feel their illness. Some delay and wait to see if their illness might improve on its own, until they become bedridden and end up in the infirmary; only then do they first have the physician called to them, present their illness to him, and ask for advice and help. Some, however, though they are dangerously ill and even confined to bed, still do not send for the physician; indeed, even if he came himself, or was brought by others to visit them and share his advice, and diligently offered everything and even comforted them—still, they do not want him, they despise him, and drive him away from them with vehemence. In such cases, the physician often takes pity on the sick person himself, notices the danger, and laments that the person will not let him help, but rather willfully neglects himself, dies, and perishes.

It happens in a very similar way with those who are spiritually ill: Some immediately seek the heavenly physician, Jesus Christ, when they recognize the sickness of their soul—that is, sin—or are overtaken by a fall wherein they become guilty before God through evil thoughts, words, or deeds. They do not let sin stay overnight nor allow the sickness of their soul to gain the upper hand; they judge...

themselves, they anticipate the judgment of God so that they are not judged; that is, immediately after their transgression, they confess their fall and illness to the Physician with repentance. For this, everyone who is holy shall pray to you at the right time." The third kind are those who are terminally ill, full of sin and vice, but who neither fear God’s judgment nor desire Christ the heavenly Physician; indeed, they want to hear nothing of their soul’s sickness. These are the godless children of the world who do not worry or care about the salvation of their souls. In this state, each person acts according to the thoughts of his evil, perverse heart. And although the Physician Jesus Christ offers himself even to such as these, and has his healing and restoration proclaimed to them through the Gospel, they despise it and push it away from themselves; indeed, they revile, blaspheme, and persecute him and his own. They remain standing firm in their evil ways and will not be shown how to do good. Of such people the Prophet also speaks in Psalm 36:2: "It is from the bottom of my..."

But some run away from the Physician’s cure, do not follow instructions, and despise the prescription for health which the Physician prescribes for them; in such cases, the last state becomes worse for them than the first, if they do not turn back again. Of these, the Apostle Peter writes: It would have been better for them not to have known the way of righteousness than to know it and turn away from the holy commandment given to them. The true proverb has happened to them: "The dog returns to what he has vomited; and the sow, after washing, returns to wallowing in the mire." 2 Peter 2:21–22.

The third group has no need for the Physician, for they consider themselves healthy, righteous, and without sin, or they help themselves with concocted false medicine of men—their own works, ceremonies, rules, etc. These are the **Scribes and Pharisees** <note>In the New Testament, these groups often represented legalistic religious authorities who opposed Jesus and relied on outward rituals rather than inner transformation.</note>, who do not come to the only true Physician, Christ, for medicine. They act as if they were healthy and did not need him, to whom the Lord says in the Gospel: "Those who are healthy do not need a physician, but those who are sick. I have come to call sinners to **repentance** <term>Buße</term><note>The German term 'Buße' implies both a change of mind and a turning away from sin toward God.</note> and not the righteous." Luke 5:31–32.

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of spiritual healing. # Chapter 18.

### Concerning the injuries in general that Christ the Physician heals.

So I will now also speak of the common afflictions of spiritual illness. Several teachers have summarized all the injuries, sins, and diseases of the soul that our Physician Jesus Christ heals into three parts: namely, first, the internal wounding and innate evil desires of the heart; second, the outward act, when one consents to the temptation and the urge of indwelling sin, so that the evil desires break out into works and are carried out in deed; third, the persistent habit, where the person sinks so deeply into sins that there appears to be almost no hope of recovery. This, as **Augustine** <note>St. Augustine of Hippo (354–430 AD), a foundational Christian theologian who often used allegorical interpretations of the Bible.</note> writes of it, was signified by the three dead persons whom the Lord Christ physically raised—namely, the little daughter, the young man, and Lazarus.

First, with the daughter of the ruler of the synagogue, whom the Lord raised in the house (Matthew 9:25), the healing of the internal sickness of sin "at home" in the heart is signified. Second, in the raising of the young man who was already being carried out of the gate (Luke 7:12), the ulcerous sore of the disease is signified, which has now broken out publicly, or the sin that manifests through works. And the third is the buried **Lazarus**, by whom the persistent plague and sickness of the soul is signified, where the person has, as it were, grown old, stubborn, stinking, and hardened in the habit of sin.

From all such injuries, Christ the heavenly Physician wishes to deliver, heal, and make whole all those who heartily desire it. For this purpose, he was also appointed and ordained by God the Father as a Physician and Savior for all corrupted people. He demonstrated this for our comfort not only through the three miracles mentioned but through all others where he healed various sick people. However, the healing of all wounds and all sins in the flesh is not completed in us all at once here; rather, for our own good, it continues as long as we live. Since, despite the health of our spirit, we nevertheless always remain somewhat weak and in part still sinners, and for that reason, we should rightly pray the petition in the **Lord’s Prayer** <term>Lord's Prayer: Referred to in the text as the "Vater Unser" (Our Father).</term>, "and forgive us our debts," all our lives until this "body of death" is finally cast off through death. Therefore, we can never do without this Physician here, but we should always honor him and keep him before our eyes, just as it is written concerning the physical physician in **Sirach 38:1–3** <note>original: "Sir. 38, 1 = 3". This refers to the Book of Sirach (Ecclesiasticus), which advises honoring physicians for their God-given skills.</note>.

<vocab>Physician Christ, sickness of the soul, Augustine, raising of the dead, habit of sin, healing, Lord's Prayer</vocab>

<summary>This page categorizes spiritual ailments into three stages—internal desire, outward action, and hardened habit—likening them to the three people Christ raised from the dead. It explains that spiritual healing is a continuous process that requires the Physician's care throughout a believer's entire life.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual illness, St. Augustine, miracles of Jesus, Lazarus, repentance, original sin, lifelong sanctification, Sirach</keywords>

--- Page 63 ---
<meta>This page continues Chapter 18, expanding on the role of Christ as the "Heavenly Physician." It follows the previous page’s categorization of spiritual injuries—using the biblical examples of the daughter of Jairus, the young man of Nain, and Lazarus—to illustrate how Christ addresses everything from internal sin to long-standing habits.</meta>

->❦❦❦❦❦<-

Christ is the faithful, perfect Physician who heals all kinds of spiritual injuries of those people who come to Him and believe in Him. He heals not only the ancestral damage or <term>original sin</term> <note>German: "Erbsünde." The theological concept that humanity inherits a sinful nature from Adam.</note>, as some formerly taught and claimed, but He also heals and forgives all other mortal and daily sins, and everything that a person has ever done wrong or possessed within themselves from youth onward. He also helps continually; when a person, through the weakness of their flesh, is overtaken by a fault—should they stray somewhat from the Physician’s prescription—He remains ready to assist. In short, the heavenly Physician helps, heals, and provides medicine as often as the poor person stands in need of comfort, salvation, and help.

And since the new Christian life does not pour into us so quickly as to reach perfect, lasting health, nor can all wounds be healed with such haste, this heavenly Physician also wishes to take His time with His patients. This is all for the patient’s own good, so that they may learn to recognize the magnitude of their mortal illness ever more clearly; likewise, that they may more diligently perceive the heartfelt faithfulness, love, and power of the Physician with gratitude, so that the Physician becomes increasingly precious to them, and the gift of beginning health becomes more pleasant, increases daily, and is preserved with greater caution.

Through this process, the one who is being made healthy also learns to have all the more patience with other sick people afterward. They look to the Physician in all things, love Him more and more, and give themselves entirely to His cure from the heart. They apply themselves all the more earnestly to keeping the rules and prescriptions of spiritual health: to die to their evil lusts and to renounce sins. The spiritual food and drink of the soul tastes better to them the longer they partake; and the stronger and healthier they become, the more the trust and love of their heart toward the Physician grows and increases. Then, such a person says along with **Paul**: *Christ is my life, just as dying is my gain* <note>A reference to Philippians 1:21: "For to me, to live is Christ, and to die is gain."</note>. At that point, on the Last Day in the resurrection of the righteous, they will attain perfect health in body and soul through Christ.

Regarding this, it is also to be noted that the Physician Christ Jesus has brought it about with **God** His Father—for all of us who obediently surrender ourselves to His <term>order of salvation</term> <note>German: "Heilsordnung." A theological term describing the steps or process by which a person receives the merits of Christ.</note> and persevere therein—that He will nevermore remember nor reckon against us all our remaining sin and sickness, which we still bear and feel with displeasure. This is promised for His sake, provided that we honor, love, and accept Him as the heavenly Physician and Son of God with true faith and obedience, and fight against sin in His grace, walking not according to the flesh but according to the spirit. This was promised in **Jeremiah 31:34** and **Hebrews 8:12**, and it is the only right kind of the new...

<summary>This page describes Christ as a patient Physician who heals both original and daily sins, explaining that the slow process of spiritual recovery is intended to build the patient's gratitude, caution, and empathy for others.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Heavenly Physician" from Chapter 18, concluding the explanation of the Covenant of Grace before transitioning into Chapter 19, which focuses on the "medicine" (the Word of God) used for spiritual healing.</meta>

...of the eternal testament, of the <term>Covenant of Grace</term> <note>German: "Gnaden-Bund." A central theological concept referring to God's promise of salvation through faith rather than through human works or the Old Testament law.</note> between God and humanity; namely, that He will be merciful toward our wickedness and sin, and will no longer remember our unrighteousness, and will even write His law in our hearts. God established this covenant with believing people through the death of His Son, and consecrated, prepared, and confirmed it through the shedding of His blood.

In short, Christ our heavenly Physician and Lord, through His unique sacrifice, removes the wounded, sick conscience of the sinner entirely before God all at once, and in its place gives a healthy, good conscience—a conscience of peace, joy, and eternal salvation. O Lord! Increase our faith, so that we may also feel such rich, unspeakable benefits in our hearts, and may serve You with a healthy, good conscience, soul, and body in eternity, Amen.

# The 19th Chapter.

### What the heavenly medicine is, with which Christ the Physician heals, and through which the sick person becomes healthy and blessed.

Since we have now heard, through the testimony of Holy Scripture, who the heavenly Physician is, who the patients of this Physician are, what the soul’s sickness is, and how it should be recognized, we must further say something about the <term>medicine</term> <note>German: "Arzeney." In this context, it refers to spiritual remedies, specifically the Word of God and the Sacraments.</note> with which this Physician heals and makes healthy. For just as the Physician is, and the patient and the illness are, so must it also be with the medicine. The Physician, however, as was said, is heavenly; that which He heals through the office of the Holy Spirit is of a spiritual nature—namely, the corrupted spirit, soul, and conscience of man. Therefore, the medicine must conversely also be of such a nature, so tempered and prepared that it can powerfully reach the spiritual illness (namely, the soul), heal the wounds of the heart, take away the disease, give a new, joyful life, and indeed introduce eternal health in place of the sickness. Such medicine is originally nothing other than the living Word of God, which became flesh, through which all things were created, preserved, and governed; through which we also, after Adam’s fall, are brought back and reborn, and through which our sins are forgiven. Of this Word it is written in the Psalm: *God sent His Word and made them healthy and rescued them, so that they should not die.* <note>Psalm 107:20</note>.

<summary>This page concludes the discussion of Christ's sacrifice as the foundation of the Covenant of Grace and introduces the Word of God as the "heavenly medicine" required to heal the human soul.</summary>
<keywords>Covenant of Grace, Christ as Physician, spiritual medicine, conscience, Word of God, Psalm 107, spiritual healing</keywords>

--- Page 65 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "heavenly medicine" introduced in Chapter 19. The previous page ended mid-word ("m..."), likely "medicine" (Arzeney). This page begins after a decorative divider, providing biblical parallels for the Word of God as a healing force.</meta>

<image-desc>A decorative horizontal divider consisting of a wavy line with small dots above and below it.</image-desc>

Thus we clearly see that the eternal Word of God, Jesus Christ, as well as His flesh and blood in the Word, is the true **medicine** <term>medicine: "Arzeney" in the original German, referring to a curative remedy or spiritual physic.</term> with which God helps, heals, and makes both body and soul healthy. The heavenly Physician proved this Himself during the days of His life on earth, when He raised the dead through the Word, drove out demons in the Word, and forgave sins. Through the Word—which is spirit and life—He healed every kind of sickness. For just as the rain and snow fall from heaven and do not return there, but instead moisten the earth, making it fertile and productive so that it gives seed to sow and bread to eat: "So shall my word be," says the Lord, "that goes out from my mouth; it shall not return to me empty, but it shall accomplish that which I purpose, and shall succeed in the thing for which I sent it." Isaiah 55:10–11.

Now, what the snow and rain do for the parched, barren earth—acting as its medicine and refreshment so that it may bring forth fruit, growth, and grain—that is exactly what the Word of the Lord is and does for the "withered human earth." It acts upon all sick sinners and all languishing, needy hearts and consciences. For as soon as God lets His Word depart from His mouth and enter our souls (for which all the sick should daily pray and call upon Him), we are made healthy from sin. Our sin is forgiven and our soul is rescued so that it does not die the death. Our heart is purified to be a dwelling place and a **temple of God** <note>This reflects the New Testament idea that the believer's body and heart become a sacred space for the divine.</note>, just as the heavenly Physician said to His disciples: "You are already clean because of the word which I have spoken to you." Therefore, the living Word of God is primarily the health-giving medicine of our souls, and the joy, delight, and comfort of our hearts. Jeremiah 15:16.

We wish here to recount the story of the **bronze serpent** <note>original: "eherne Schlange." A famous biblical "type" or symbolic foreshadowing of Christ's crucifixion.</note> in the wilderness as a **figure** <term>figure: "Figur," a theological term meaning a symbolic historical event that foreshadows a later spiritual truth.</term>, so that the aforementioned medicine may be better understood, and likewise, so that the nature of the sickness may be more clearly recognized. It is written in Numbers 21:5–6: After the Jewish people spoke against God and Moses, the Lord sent fiery serpents among the people, and they bit the people so that many in Israel died. Then they came to Moses and said: "We have sinned, for we have spoken against the Lord and against you; pray to the Lord that He take away the serpents from us." Moses prayed for the people. Then the Lord said to Moses: "Make a bronze serpent and set it up as a sign: whoever is bitten and looks at it shall live." So Moses made a bronze serpent and set it up as a sign, and whenever a serpent bit someone, he looked at the bronze serpent and lived.

From this figure, you see once again the poisonous bite of the ancient serpent, the evil spirit’s initial whispering, and

<summary>This page identifies the Word of God as the "heavenly medicine" that heals the soul, using the biblical analogies of rain falling on dry earth and the healing of the Israelites through the bronze serpent.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the theological explanation of the "heavenly medicine." It moves from the general metaphor of rain and snow (from the previous page) to the specific biblical typology of the bronze serpent in the wilderness, framing it as a precursor to Christ’s healing power over human corruption.</meta>

the corruption of our nature, by which the serpent severely wounded our first parents in Paradise in their soul, heart, and conscience, introducing sin, death, disobedience toward God, contempt for eternal life, and all misfortune. It was because of this same innate sickness of sin and the corruption of their flesh that the children of Israel also murmured impatiently against God and His servant Moses, being disobedient, bitter, and contrary toward him.

God wished to depict this innate inner poison and deadly plague through the specific symbol of the bite of the fiery serpents, thereby giving a visual representation of the evil that dwells within the human heart. At the same time, He intended to teach them, through the raising of the **bronze serpent** <term>bronze serpent: Referring to the "Nehushtan" from Numbers 21. In Christian tradition, this event is a "typology"—a person or event in the Old Testament that foreshadows something in the New Testament (in this case, the Crucifixion).</term>, that His eternal divine Word—which has now come into the flesh as Jesus Christ—would atone for and blot out the bite of the fiery serpent (that is, Satan’s poison) upon the cross. Exalted, transfigured, and glorified in God through His flesh and blood, He was to be the only wholesome **medicine** <term>medicine: "Arzeney," the central theme of this chapter, referring to spiritual healing as a "physic" for the soul.</term> for the aforementioned deadly plague or sickness, now and forevermore. This was so that all who recognized their sickness (as they did when they said: "we have sinned") and accepted the Word of God with the faith of their hearts, might look upon the crucified and now exalted Christ in faith as their help and life. Thus, He would free those who were obedient to His command and counsel from the bite of the ancient serpent—that is, from all their sin and sickness—and make their souls entirely whole and healthy.

That it should be understood in this way is also found in the **Book of Wisdom** <note>Book of Wisdom: Also known as the Wisdom of Solomon, a book of the Apocrypha. The author uses it here to provide a bridge between the Old Testament Law and the New Testament Gospel.</note>, where it is written: "Those who turned toward that symbol were made healthy, not by what they gazed upon, but by You, the Savior of all." And shortly after, even more clearly: "Neither herb nor plaster healed them, but Your Word, O Lord, which heals all things." (Wisdom 16:7, 12). Thus Holy Scripture testifies that God and His Word are the Physician and Healer. From this it follows, first of all, that Christ is indisputably God, just as His Word is the original true medicine because it heals all things; for this reason, it is also called the Word of Salvation. This agrees with what was previously said about this heavenly medicine, where David says: "God sent His word and made them healthy, and delivered them, so that they did not die." <note>This refers to Psalm 107:20.</note>

# Chapter 20.

### How the Word of God, Jesus Christ, is the Physician and the Medicine Himself; and How His Flesh and Blood in the Word Heal Our Sick Soul, Making it Righteous and Healthy.

This is now the wholesome, lovely **medicine** <term>medicine: "Arzeney" in the original German, referring to a spiritual remedy or "physic" that restores the soul to health.</term> which is presented and offered for free to all the sick by Christ. This medicine should be well and properly recognized by all wounded consciences and all poor, burdened people, and they should desire it from the heart. It is, as has been heard, originally and primarily the eternal, living Word of God, in which we have the forgiveness of sins, the health of our souls, and eternal life. In the fullness of time, specifically for the healing of mankind and for the sake of our salvation—to fulfill the aforementioned **figure** <term>figure: A "typology" or symbolic foreshadowing where an Old Testament event (like the bronze serpent) points toward a New Testament reality (Christ).</term>—it was sent into the world by God and became flesh and man. 

In summary, as previously mentioned, this is nothing other than Jesus Christ, the Son of the eternal Father, through whom God the Father created mankind in the beginning. And after man had fallen into a deadly sickness through sin, transgression, and disobedience, he was also rescued again through Him—He who forgives man’s sin so that he does not die the eternal death, but rather lives and becomes fresh, joyful, and healthy. For this, one should thank the Lord for His goodness and for His wonders that He performs for the children of men; one should offer the sacrifice of thanksgiving and declare His works with joy. <note>original citation: "Ps. 107, 8. 15."</note>

Thus, now that Jesus Christ has been exalted on the cross, the fulfillment of the figure of the exalted bronze serpent has also been accomplished for our true medicine and recovery. This was signified back in that time by the setting up of the bronze serpent, of which the Lord Himself says in John 3:14–15: "Just as Moses lifted up a serpent in the wilderness, so must the Son of Man be lifted up; so that all who believe in Him should not perish, but have eternal life." 

Through this interpretation, we thoroughly and fundamentally understand what God ultimately intends when He says: "Whoever is bitten and looks at it (the serpent) shall live." We see how all things were directed toward Jesus Christ and His restoration, help, and incarnation. It is the eternal Word of God, the incarnate Word of Life, Jesus Christ, who was first set up on the cross and now, through His cross and suffering, is exalted by God upon the heavenly throne according to His humanity. He is to be sought, looked upon, and recognized with the faith of the heart at the right hand of God, as the remedy for the deadly wound and bite of the old serpent; there He [heals] our...

7:33 <note>original: "7, 33"; refers to Mark 7:33, where Jesus heals a deaf man by touching his tongue with saliva</note>; when he made the blind man see, he spat in his eyes, Mark 8:23; furthermore, when he spat on the ground and made clay <note>original: "Koth," literally "filth" or "mud," used here in the biblical sense of clay or dirt</note> from the saliva and smeared it on the blind man’s eyes, and commanded him to wash in Siloam, John 9:6-7. But so that no one here may go astray, it should be noted: although this heavenly medicine and the richness of its power and divine effect are named and witnessed in Holy Scripture by many different names (some of which have been recounted), it is fundamentally only one single divine medicine for the healing of everything that has been corrupted in man’s body and soul by the evil spirit. It is to be found in Christ Jesus alone and in his Spirit of Grace; for the Holy Spirit (although Christ is the Physician) shall not be separated from Christ in power, strength, and office. Indeed, it is Christ—as has been heard many times—who is both the Physician and the medicine itself in the Spirit of Grace. But because of the manifold sicknesses, damages, wounds, and festering boils of mankind, this medicine also possesses manifold powers and innumerable effects and capabilities, so that it can help, heal, and be used for all the boils, wounds, and stings of sin, death, hell, and the evil spirit.

# Chapter 21.

### Why the heavenly Physician is named Jesus Christ; what is to be held and believed concerning him: and also what the confession of the true Christian faith consists of.

Therefore it is that our heavenly Physician is named **Jesus Christ**; firstly, he is called Jesus (a Savior, Redeemer, Rescuer, Healer <note>original: "Seligmacher," literally "one who makes blessed" or "savior"</note>) because of the healing he brings, as it is written of this in the Gospel: His name shall be called Jesus, for he will make his people whole <note>original: "heil machen"; in German, the word for "healing" (Heilung) and "salvation" (Heil) share the same root, reinforcing the "Physician" metaphor</note> from their sins—that is, from the sickness of their souls. Regarding this, Peter also speaks to the Jews in the Acts of the Apostles, saying: The God of our fathers raised up Jesus, whom you murdered and hanged on a tree; him God has exalted by his right hand to be a Prince and Savior, to give repentance to Israel and forgiveness of sins, *Acts 5:30-31*. Take note here of what a Savior is: how, when, and through what he became a Savior; then you will soon discover the office of our heavenly Physician, Jesus, among his patients, just as the sickness and the medicine may be understood from the resulting repentance and forgiveness of sins.

Furthermore, our Physician is not only called Jesus, but he is also named Christ (Anointed One) because of the glory of his humanity stemming from the divine anointing. As the Anointed of God, even according to the flesh, he has now entered into God on behalf of all other people, of which Peter says to the Jews in *Acts 2:36*: Therefore, let the whole house of Israel know for certain that God has made this Jesus, whom you crucified, both Lord and Christ; as if he wanted to say: God has completely transfigured <term>transfigured: transformed into a more beautiful or spiritual state</term> this man, whom you esteemed so little and crucified; he has made him equal to Himself in essence, power, honor, and glory. He has poured out his divine ointment and balm upon him entirely, inside and out—yes, filled and saturated him with the oil of joy of the Holy Spirit, so that it remains not only in him as the Head, but also flows down from his head into the beard and the hem of his garment <note>a reference to Psalm 133, describing the anointing of the High Priest Aaron as a symbol of unity and blessing</note>—that is, into the members of his body and into all Christian-believing hearts. Thus, Jesus Christ himself, in the Holy Spirit, is the ointment, the scent, and the lovely medicine, by which all other people are not only healed, renewed, and cleansed from various deadly wounds and the ulcers of sin, but are also anointed, sanctified, and adorned, so that in him they may become a sweet aroma of God, of divine nature, spirit, and life—yes, and may partake eternally in the Kingdom of Heaven.

<summary>This page explains the theological titles of Jesus Christ through the lens of a physician, linking the name 'Jesus' to the act of healing souls and 'Christ' to the divine anointing that flows from him to his followers.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the theological exploration of Christ as the "Heavenly Physician," specifically addressing why he used physical elements like saliva and touch in his miracles, and clarifying the nature of the "medicine" he provides.</meta>

7:33; when he made the blind man see, he spat in his eyes, Mark 8:23; furthermore, when he spat on the ground and made mud from the saliva and smeared it on the blind man’s eyes, and told him to wash in Siloam, John 9:6-7. Now, although Christ could have accomplished all this through his powerful word alone, he nevertheless wished to use the help and fellowship of his holy body—which is united with the Word—in doing so. Thus, he did not touch the deathly ill and corrupted flesh in vain for its restoration to health, but through this action, he signaled the mystery of his Incarnation, or rather, the future exaltation and divinization <note>original: "Gottgleichwerdung," a term referring to the process of humanity becoming "like God" or the concept of *theosis* through Christ</note> of humanity within him. In this way, his human nature—by the power of the Word, just as the Word (or divine nature) itself was inseparably united with it—should become the ointment and medicine <note>original: "Arzney," an archaic spelling of *Arznei*, meaning medicine or physic</note> for our souls, helping and powerfully contributing to all manner of spiritual health.

However, so that no one here may go astray, it should be noted: although the heavenly medicine and the richness of its power and divine effect are named and testified to in Holy Scripture by many different names (some of which have been mentioned), it is fundamentally only a single divine medicine for the healing of everything in man’s body and soul that has been corrupted by the evil spirit. This medicine is to be found alone in Christ Jesus and in his Spirit of grace. Just as the Holy Spirit should not be separated from Christ in power, strength, and office (even though Christ is the Physician)—indeed, as we have heard several times, Christ is both the Physician and the medicine itself in the Spirit of grace—so too, because of man’s manifold illnesses, injuries, wounds, and festering sores, this medicine also possesses manifold power and countless effects and abilities. Thus, it can help, heal, and be used for all boils, wounds, and stings of sin, death, hell, and the evil spirit.

# Chapter 21.

### Why the Heavenly Physician is Named Jesus Christ; What is to be Held and Believed Concerning Him; and What the Confession of the True Christian Faith Is.

Therefore it is also that our heavenly physician is named **Jesus Christ**; first, he is called Jesus (a Savior, Redeemer, Rescuer, Bringer of Salvation) <note>original: "Heyland, Erlöser, Erretter, Seligmacher." These terms all emphasize the act of making someone "whole" or "safe."</note> because of the healing he brings, as it is written of him in the Gospel: His name shall be called Jesus, for he will make his people whole <note>original: "heil machen," meaning to heal or make sound</note> from their sins, that is, from the sickness of their souls. Regarding this, Peter also speaks of him to the Jews in the Acts of the Apostles, saying: The God of our fathers raised up Jesus, whom you slew and hung on a tree; him has God exalted with his right hand to be a Prince and a Savior, to give Israel repentance and forgiveness of sins. Acts 5:30-31. Pay attention here to what a Savior is: how? when? and through what he became a Savior; thus you will soon discover the office of our heavenly physician, Jesus, among his patients, just as the sickness and the medicine may be understood afterward through repentance and the forgiveness of sins.

Furthermore, our physician is not only called Jesus, but he is also named Christ (Anointed One) because of the glory of his humanity stemming from the divine anointing. As the Anointed of God, even according to the flesh, he has now become [the standard] for all other men in God. Concerning this, Peter says to the Jews in Acts 2:36: Therefore let the whole house of Israel know for certain that God has made this Jesus, whom you crucified, both Lord and Christ. It is as if he wanted to say: God has fully transfigured <note>original: "verkläret," often used to describe the Transfiguration, implying a change into a more glorious, divine state</note> this man, whom you esteemed so little and crucified; He has made him equal to Himself in essence, power, honor, and glory. He has completely showered him, inside and out, with His divine ointment and balsam; indeed, He has filled and saturated him with the oil of gladness of the Holy Spirit. This was done so that it remains not only in him, as in the head, but also flows down from his head into the beard and the hem of his garment—that is, flows out into the members of his body and into all hearts that believe in Christ. Thus, Jesus Christ himself, in the Holy Spirit, is the ointment, the fragrance, and the lovely medicine through which all other people are not only healed, renewed, and cleansed from various deadly wounds and the sores of sin, but are also anointed, sanctified, and adorned. In him, they may eternally partake of the sweet fragrance of God, of the divine nature, spirit, and life—yes, even of the kingdom of heaven.

# Chapter 21.

### Why the heavenly Physician is called Jesus Christ; what is to be held and believed concerning him: and what the confession of the true Christian faith consists of.

Therefore it is also that our heavenly physician is called **Jesus Christ**; first, he is called **Jesus** (a Savior, Redeemer, Rescuer, Blessed-maker) on account of his work of healing, just as it is written of him in the Gospel: His name shall be called Jesus, for he will make whole <note>original: "heil machen," meaning both to save and to heal</note> his people from their sins—that is, from their soul's sickness. Regarding this, Peter also speaks of him to the Jews in the Acts of the Apostles, saying: The God of our fathers raised up Jesus, whom you murdered and hanged on a tree; Him God has exalted by His right hand to be a Prince and a Savior, to give repentance to Israel and forgiveness of sins (Acts 5:30-31). Note well here what a Savior is: how, when, and by what means he became a Savior; thus you will soon discover the office of our heavenly physician Jesus toward his sick ones, just as both the sickness and the medicine may be understood from the resulting repentance and forgiveness of sins.

Furthermore, our physician is called not only Jesus, but he is also called **Christ** (the Anointed One) because of the glory of his humanity stemming from the divine anointing. As the Anointed of God, even according to the flesh, he has now become the way for all other men into God. Regarding this, Peter says to the Jews in Acts 2:36: Therefore let all the house of Israel know for certain that God has made this Jesus, whom you crucified, both Lord and Christ. It is as if he wanted to say: God has fully transfigured this man, whom you esteemed so little and crucified, making him equal to Himself in essence, power, honor, and glory. He has poured over him, inside and out, His divine ointment and balsam—indeed, He has filled and permeated him with the Oil of Gladness <note>original: "Freuden-Oele," a reference to the anointing of the Holy Spirit</note> of the Holy Spirit. This was done so that the anointing remains not only in him, as in the Head, but also flows down from his head into the beard and the hem of his garment—that is, into the members of his body and into all Christian-believing hearts. Thus, Jesus Christ himself, in the Holy Spirit, is the ointment, the scent, and the lovely medicine itself. By this, all other people are not only healed, renewed, and cleansed from various deadly wounds and the sores of sin, but are also anointed, sanctified, and adorned, so that in him, they may become a sweet fragrance of God, partaking eternally of the divine nature, Spirit, and life—indeed, of the Kingdom of Heaven.

<meta>This page continues the theological exposition of Christ as the "Heavenly Physician," moving from his titles of Jesus and Christ to the practical application of his "medicine" through the Holy Spirit and the ministry of the Apostle Paul. Thus, Christ Jesus is made for us by God for wisdom and for righteousness, for sanctification and for redemption (1 Corinthians 1:30); yes, certainly also for medicine and restoration to health, for the oil of joy and anointing. The sweet, lovely name of Jesus is a poured-out oil, medicine, or ointment. By the word "Name," the essence, power, and ability of the one so named is to be understood; one should not persist outwardly in the word and sound of the name, but rather penetrate through faith to the one whose name it is.

For this reason, Paul was chosen and accepted as an apostle or servant of the heavenly physician, so that he might make known to the whole world this name—the divine strength, power, honor, and glory of Christ, which he also received according to his humanity—so that he might present this ointment or medicine to all people through the proclamation of his service. As Christ, the heavenly physician himself, testifies when he speaks to Ananias: "Go, for he is a chosen vessel <note>original: "Werkzeug," meaning tool or instrument</note> unto me, to bear my name before the Gentiles, and kings, and the children of Israel" (Acts 9:15), about which we shall hear further.

For there is salvation in no other; there is no other name given to men by which we may become healthy, holy, and blessed. "His name," speaks the Bride <note>A traditional interpretation of the beloved in the Song of Solomon as the Church or the soul</note> of the heavenly physician concerning her Bridegroom Jesus Christ, "is a poured-out ointment" (he heals and helps evermore); therefore the young maidens—that is, the souls who believe in Christ—love him because of such a lovely ointment of the Holy Spirit, which is poured into them from his name, whereby he heals them, gladdens them, and makes them healthy in their hearts, souls, and consciences. From him, all true Christians, as the anointed of the Lord, derive their name, strength, and origin. The sick are then called "Christians" for the sake of the medicine and the physician as soon as he shares with them—through grace in the Holy Spirit—his eternal health, the scent of his ointments and salvation, indeed, himself. The scent of his ointments surpasses all the spices of the earth (Song of Solomon 4:10). Truly, his clothes are pure myrrh, aloes, and cassia (Psalm 45:8). From this, a most lovely scent goes forth into the parched hearts of all believers; this is the Word of grace, the Word of salvation and life, the Gospel of peace, which brings the lovely, enduring health more and more into the hearts of all sick Christians.

This then is also called the true Christian faith: namely, to believe and confess that Jesus is the Christ (the Lord) and Son of God, through which faith alone we have life (that is, health, healing, and blessedness) in his name.

# The 22nd Chapter.

### Concerning the Glory and Splendor of the Being of our Heavenly Physician Jesus Christ.

Regarding this man, our heavenly Physician Jesus Christ, his suffering and death, and thereafter also his exaltation, kingdom, and glory, nearly all of Scripture and many Psalms bear witness—especially Psalm 45, where he is called by the Prophet David the most beautiful among all people, a King of honors, indeed God with his divine ornament, decoration, and splendor. Among other things, the Prophet speaks thus: "You love righteousness and hate godless ways; therefore God, your God, has anointed you with the oil of joy more than your companions" <note>Psalm 45:7. The author emphasizes the title "Christ," which means "The Anointed One."</note>. See, there the Prophet calls him God, and testifies in the Spirit how God his Father has anointed him with the oil of joy without any measure—that is, with the fullness of the entire Godhead. This is now the ointment and the oil because of which our Physician is called *Christ*, as we have said before.

Just as oil also has three properties—it gives light, it nourishes, and it heals—so also does our anointed King Jesus Christ enlighten, nourish, and heal. For he is the sun and the light of the entire world, without which all people walk in the darkness of death. Of him it is written in the Prophet Malachi: "But for you who fear my name, the Sun of Righteousness (Christ) shall rise, and healing and health are under his wings" <note>Malachi 4:2. The author uses the brackets to explicitly identify the "Sun" as Christ.</note>. This sun brings the bright, blissful day of eternity with it and shall never set. Likewise, he is the food of the children of God, without which all hungry souls would waste away—of which the Lord himself taught abundantly in John 6. Furthermore, he is also the ointment and true medicine, as has been heard, without which all the sick—that is, all people—must die the eternal death and perish.

I have wished to show this regarding the exaltation, power, strength, and glory of Jesus Christ and his holy humanity—our heavenly Physician—in addition to what was said previously, so that the sick may know how to rightly honor such a Physician and Savior, and likewise the medicine of his holy flesh and blood, and hold him high and glorious in their hearts. This they will surely do when they [perceive] the new heavenly divine being in Christ...

It draws on Psalm 45 and Malachi 4 to establish Christ as the "Sun of Righteousness" and the source of eternal health.</summary>
