This page continues that argument using the prophecies of Ezekiel and the teachings of Jesus and Paul.</meta>

Otherwise, without this, we are before God poor, unfit, corrupt, and dead people; indeed, we are like the dried-out bones in which the Lord promises to bring breath, so that they shall become alive. <note>original: "Ezech. 37"</note> Just as the merciful, kind God has also graciously promised to make us fit for His true worship and to give us His Spirit, where He says: I will put my Spirit within you and will make such people of you who walk in my commandments, and keep my laws and act accordingly. Upon this, the promise of eternal life follows, as He further speaks: And you shall dwell in the land that I gave to your fathers (namely Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob), and shall be my people, and I will be your God, etc. Then you will remember your evil life and your deeds, which were not good, and you will repent of your sin and idolatry, says the Lord. <note>original: "Ezech. 36, 26, 27"</note> To this, Paul also afterward looked in the Epistle to the Romans and comfortingly explained that all such things would be fulfilled in and through Christ in the believers, as was previously promised. It is also found from the mentioned saying of the prophet that without the Spirit of Grace, no one may rightly repent of his sin nor truly recognize it, much less serve God uprightly in a new life.

Therefore, true worship is now that which comes from God Himself through Christ, when He writes His living law into our heart and mind, when we serve God in spirit and in truth (which is Christ). Concerning this, the Lord also says in the Gospel: The time is coming and is already here when the true worshippers (which is the highest form of worship) will worship the Father in spirit and in truth, for the Father also wants to have those who worship Him so. God is a spirit, and those who worship Him must worship him in spirit and in truth. <note>original: "Joh. 4, 21"</note>

Here also the Lord’s Prayer <term>Vater Unser: literally "Our Father," the standard German name for the prayer Jesus taught his disciples.</term> belongs, in which the Lord teaches us to ask for true worship—namely, that the name of God be hallowed in us, that His kingdom come to us, that His will be done through us, etc. From this, we may clearly see that the hallowing of God’s name, the knowledge of His kingdom, and the fulfillment of His will—indeed, true worship—originally flows from God through Christ in His Spirit. It is likewise accomplished and summarized primarily in the knowledge of Christ, which is according to the Spirit, and in faith. This is not a "historical," dead faith <note>meaning a mere intellectual belief in facts without personal transformation</note>, but a true living faith, which is a participation in the gift of God’s very essence and is fulfilled in Christ. Therefore, the Lord Christ also says to His disciples: Without me you can do nothing—that is, without me you cannot bear fruit, nor serve God in a way that pleases Him.

<summary>This page argues that true worship is impossible for "spiritually dead" humans without God's direct intervention. Using Ezekiel and the Gospel of John, the author explains that God must provide His Spirit and write His law on the heart for any service to be valid.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of true worship as a divine act within the believer. Behold, thus we see again evidently how it stands with the good works that serve God’s service, through which our works also become pleasing to God, and from whence they have their origin. Also what the Lord Christ means by this when He speaks: Truly, truly I say to you, he who believes in me will also do the works that I do, and will do greater than these, etc. <note>original: "Joh. 14, 12"</note> For whoever believes in Christ and rightly recognizes Him, within them—through faith and this recognition—is the nature, essence <note>original: "Selbststand"</note>, and the Spirit of Christ; indeed, Christ works in them, so that the believing person also co-operates in Christ through His grace, which is then the only accepted and well-pleasing worship.

But to true worship <note>original: "Gottesdienste"</note>, the three aforementioned types of spiritual sacrifices about which Paul speaks also belong: namely, the sacrifice of praise, the sacrifice of benevolence, and the sacrifice of our body (which sacrifice consists in the denial of oneself and the death of the "old Adam"). <note>In the theology of this period, the "old Adam" refers to the fallen, sinful human nature that must "die" to allow the new life in Christ to grow.</note> All of this God and our Lord Christ work primarily in us, after He has renewed, awakened, and moved our mind and will to His service; indeed, He pours into our hearts that which belongs to Him for serving Him, provided we do not merely endure His work in us, but voluntarily co-operate in His power of grace and obediently surrender ourselves to His service. This is so that He may either work something good through us for His praise and the neighbor’s benefit, or else take away the sin that displeases Him in us, granting that we hate it from the heart, in place of which He plants righteousness, His gifts, and His good pleasure in us through the Holy Spirit, making us fit for His kingdom.

All this belongs to the service of God, with which we serve Him in Christ our Lord for our self-edification and development in the newness of the spirit; otherwise, without Christ and His Spirit, everything is hypocrisy, wind, fables, self-seeking, and human effort, even if one thinks one is serving God in the very best way. Just as we can have a good conscience in worship only through Jesus Christ and His calling and movement, so too are we prepared, anointed, sealed, and confirmed for eternal salvation <note>original: "Seligkeit"</note> only in Him and through His Spirit. <note>original: "2. Cor. 1, 21, 22"</note>

# Chapter 36.

### On the Pure Grain Offering of True Worship in the New Testament. Malachi 1.

From all of this, it is now easy to recognize what the pure grain offering <term>grain offering: "Speise-Opfer" in the German, referring to the non-animal sacrifices of the Old Testament which symbolized dedication and thankfulness to God</term>, and also the incense is, which should be offered in the true worship of the New Testament in all places. God speaks of this through the prophet Malachi, saying that He will abolish the grain offerings of the Jews—that is, the ceremonial worship of the Old Testament—and in its place will bring forth and establish a much better worship and grain offering, indeed the only true, righteous worship of the New Testament and the pure grain offering that alone counts before God. For the Lord of Hosts speaks: I have no pleasure in you, and the grain offering from your hands is not acceptable to me; but from the rising of the sun to its setting, my name shall be great among the nations, and in every place incense shall be burned to my name and a pure grain offering shall be offered. <note>original: "Malach. 1"</note>

Namely, that such a pure grain offering, which is to be offered in all places to the glorious name of God—which is Jesus Christ—consists neither of ceremonies nor of any outward gesture. Instead, it is the believing, reborn heart of man, which is reconciled and redeemed through the sacrifice of Christ, and purified and sanctified from sins through the sprinkling of His blood. Henceforth, it is to serve God holily in the newness of the spirit with a Christian life, which is also the only righteous worship, as has been heard. From this, the incense (namely, the sacrifice of praise and prayer, or the sacrifice of the Eucharist <term>Eucharist: derived from the Greek "eucharistia," meaning thanksgiving</term>, which is called a thanksgiving) reaches God as a sweet aroma. This should be the daily incense every morning before the Lord, as is written in the second book of Moses, Chapter 30, verses 7 and 8 <note>Exodus 30:7-8</note>, of which the Prophet also speaks in the Psalm: Let my prayer be directed to You (O God) like an incense offering, the lifting up of my hands like an evening sacrifice. <note>original: "Psalm 141, 2"</note> Luke 17:20: The kingdom of God does not come with outward gestures, etc.

Of such a pure grain offering and its preparation, the prophet Isaiah prophesied in Chapter 66, verse 20, where he speaks of the revelation of the glory of Christ and His church, saying: And they (namely, the sent Apostles) shall bring all your brothers from among all the nations as a grain offering to the Lord, on horses and in chariots, in litters and on mules and dromedaries to Jerusalem to my holy mountain, says the Lord, just as the children of Israel bring grain offerings in a clean vessel to the house of the Lord.

These are they who are cleansed and sanctified by the promised pure water mentioned in <note>original: "Ezechiel. 36, 25"</note> Ezekiel 36:25; indeed, they are accepted as God’s own people to serve Him. It was to be offered by many and burned as incense to the name of the Lord. All of this is fulfilled and confirmed through the sacrifice, glorification, and the coming-into-glory of Jesus Christ, when the Spirit of God was poured out over all flesh, and the law of life was given into the hearts of the believers and written in their minds. Through this, true Christians and "new humans" in Christ, as members of His body, have become a pure grain offering and a sweet fragrance to God the Lord. Therefore, Saint Paul desires that men—indeed all Christians—should pray in all places and lift up pure or holy hands. <note>original: "1 Timoth. 2. 8"</note>

Such a pure grain offering was beautifully prefigured in <note>original: "3. Buch Mose, Cap. 2, 14 = 16"</note> Leviticus 2:14–16 by the fine flour that was moistened with oil and had frankincense placed upon it, as well as by the new first fruits parched by fire or baked in the oven and crushed small. For the oil poured upon the fine flour signifies the anointing and outpouring of the Holy Spirit into all cleansed, Christian-believing hearts. The fruits parched by the fire signify the dying away and the consuming of sinful <term>passions: "Affect," referring to the disordered emotional impulses or "affects" of the fallen human nature</term> and the "sap" of the flesh in the furnace of tribulation, suffering, or the Cross. It represents a heart crushed through repentance, remorse, and sorrow, of which the prophet says in <note>The author cites Psalm 51:19, following a different numbering system; in modern Bibles, this is Psalm 51:17</note> Psalm 51:19: "The sacrifices that please God are a distressed spirit; a distressed and crushed heart, O God, You will not despise." Concerning this, the Lord Christ also says in the Gospel: "Blessed are those who bear sorrow, for they shall be comforted." <note>original: "Matth. 5, 4"</note>

# Chapter 36.

### Concerning the Pure Grain Offering of True Worship in the New Testament. Malachi 1.

From all of this, it is now easy to recognize what the pure grain offering, as well as the incense, might be, which was to be offered in every place in the true worship of the New Testament. Of this, God speaks through the prophet Malachi, stating that He will abolish the grain offerings of the Jews—that is, the ceremonial worship of the Old Testament—and in its place, He will bring forth and establish a much better worship and grain offering, indeed the one true, righteous worship of the New Testament and the pure grain offering which alone is valid before God. Malachi 1.

Namely, that such a pure grain offering, which is to be offered in every place to the glorious name of God—which is Jesus Christ—is not a matter of ceremonies or any outward gesture. Instead, it is the believing, reborn heart of man, which is reconciled and redeemed through the sacrifice of Christ, and is purified and sanctified from sins through the sprinkling of His blood, to henceforth serve God holily in the newness of the spirit with a Christian life. Psalm 141:2. Luke 17:20: The kingdom of God does not come with outward gestures, etc.

Concerning such a pure grain offering and its preparation, the prophet Isaiah prophesied in Chapter 66:20, where he speaks of the revelation of the glory of Christ and His church, saying: And they (namely the apostles who were sent out) shall bring all your brothers out of all the Gentiles as a grain offering to the Lord, on horses and wagons, on litters and mules and swift runners to Jerusalem to my holy mountain, says the Lord, just as the children of Israel bring grain offerings in a pure vessel to the house of the Lord.

--- Page 209 ---
<meta>This page continues the exposition of Chapter 36, further developing the metaphor of the "pure grain offering" as the spiritual transformation of the believer, drawing on the Apostle Paul's writings and Old Testament types.</meta>

To this end, the Apostle Paul writes in Romans 15:16—as was also mentioned before—that he served so that the Gentiles might become a sacrifice pleasing to God. This includes all people who turn to God, who are brought to the obedience of faith through the Gospel of Christ and consecrated as a temple of God through the Holy Spirit. These are also the ones purified and sanctified by the promised "pure water" mentioned in Ezekiel 36:25, and accepted as God’s own people to serve Him. Regarding this, God speaks through the prophet Malachi: "For my name (which was the promised Messiah, Christ Jesus) shall be glorious among the Gentiles." And as the Psalm says: "All kings will worship him, all Gentiles will serve him," etc. "They will give him gold from Arabia and pray for him; daily they will praise him." (Psalm 72:11, 15).

Behold, this is now the pure grain offering <note>original: "Speißopfer," a term for a cereal or meal offering, often involving flour or grain rather than animal sacrifice</note> which was to be established through Christ—not only in Israel, nor in the land of the Jews, or in Jerusalem, or by a few as in former times, but in all places of the world. It was to be offered by many and burned as incense to the name of the Lord. All of this was fulfilled and confirmed through the sacrifice, glorification, and the "becoming-glorious" of Jesus Christ, when the Spirit of God was poured out over all flesh, and the law of life was given into the hearts of believers and written in their minds. Through this, true Christians and "new men" in Christ—as members of His body—have become a pure grain offering and a sweet aroma to God the Lord. Accordingly, Saint Paul desires that men, indeed all Christians in all places, should pray and lift up "pure or holy hands." (1 Timothy 2:8).

This pure grain offering was beautifully prefigured in the third book of Moses <note>The Book of Leviticus, Chapter 2:14–16</note> by the fine flour that was drizzled with oil and had frankincense placed upon it, as well as by the new firstfruits parched at the fire or baked in the oven and crushed small. For the oil poured onto the fine flour signifies the anointing and outpouring of the Holy Spirit into all purified, Christian-believing hearts. The fruits parched at the fire signify the dying away and the consuming of sinful passions and the "juices" of the flesh in the furnace of tribulation, suffering, or the cross. It represents a heart broken through repentance, regret, and sorrow, of which the prophet says in Psalm 51:19: "The sacrifices that please God are a troubled spirit; a troubled and a broken heart, O God, you will not despise." Concerning this, the Lord Christ also says in the Gospel: "Blessed are those who mourn, for they shall be comforted." (Matthew 5:4).

But here it should be noted that although we have considered the figure of the grain offering of fine flour and of the new parched...

<summary>This page interprets the "pure grain offering" of the New Testament as the spiritual consecration of believers, particularly Gentiles, through the Holy Spirit. It links Old Testament sacrificial laws (Leviticus) and prophecies (Malachi, Ezekiel) to the inner transformation and "broken heart" of the Christian life.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the theological exposition of the "pure grain offering," shifting from its general definition to its specific symbolism regarding the life and dual nature of Christ, as well as the moral expectations for the believer.</meta>

Regarding the fruits, which represent Christians and reborn "new men," we certainly do not wish to exclude Christ—who is the perfection of all sacrifices, just as He gave Himself up for us as a sacrifice and a sweet-smelling aroma to God (Ephesians 5:2). In this way, the fruits dried by fire or baked in the oven primarily point to Christ’s first state: His suffering on the cross and His humiliation. The fine flour <note>original: "Semmelmehl," referring to the finest, highest quality wheat flour used for sacred offerings</note> poured with oil prefigured Christ in His second state: in His glory, after He received the promise of the Holy Spirit from the Father, and once He had become the food of eternal life.

And this is the reason why a handful of the grain offering <note>original: "Speißopfer"</note> is burned on the altar as a memorial, while the rest was to belong to Aaron and his sons; it shall be the "Most Holy" of the Lord’s offerings. This signifies that Christ Jesus, now in His second state <note>This refers to Christ's state of exaltation and glory following His resurrection, as opposed to His "first state" of earthly suffering.</note>, has truly and perfectly become useful to us for our sanctification, wisdom, righteousness, and—as has been heard—for the food of eternal life (1 Corinthians 1:30). Everything from Him, as the Head, reaches the Christian believers as the members of His body, through which they also become a pure grain offering and a sweet aroma to God.

Likewise, the grain offering was to be made unleavened or without yeast, which Saint Paul explains to the Corinthians when he says: "Clean out the old leaven, that you may be a new lump of dough (that is, a pure grain offering), just as you are unleavened or like fine flour. For Christ, our Passover lamb, has been sacrificed for us. Therefore let us keep the feast, not with the old leaven, nor with the leaven of malice and wickedness, but with the unleavened bread <note>original: "Süßteig," literally "sweet dough," referring to unleavened bread which has not soured from fermentation</note> of sincerity and truth" (1 Corinthians 5:7-8). Here Paul clearly explains what the "leaven" is which the grain offerings must be without: that Christians should serve God in the purity of conscience with a pure Christian life, free from all falsehood and deceit. They should walk in all godliness and the practice of good works, with prayers of praise and thanks before God, if they wish to be a pure grain offering and a sweet aroma to God.

Thus we have now spoken of the pure grain offering, which in the New Testament was to be offered to God the Almighty Lord as a sweet aroma—not only by the Jews, but also by the Gentiles—after the heavenly High Priest, Jesus Christ, sanctified and offered Himself for His believers, so that they too might be sanctified in the truth (John 17:19).

<summary>This page explains the symbolic meaning of the Old Testament grain offering, linking its preparation (baking, flour, and oil) to Christ's journey from suffering to glory. It also interprets the requirement for "unleavened" bread as a mandate for Christians to live lives of sincerity and truth, free from the "leaven" of malice.</summary>

<keywords>grain offering, Speißopfer, Christ's humiliation, Christ's glory, sanctification, unleavened bread, 1 Corinthians 5:8, High Priest, New Testament, malice and wickedness</keywords>

-> ❧❧❧❧❧ <-

Moses designates all sacrifices of the Old Testament, which the Jewish people brought into the sanctuary, as holy as soon as they came into the hands of the priests and were given to God; through these, then, when the priest offered them for the children of Israel, the people were also sanctified. However, this was only a figurative holiness <note>original: "figürliche Heiligkeit"; meaning a symbolic or representative holiness rather than a literal inward transformation</note>; for in truth, such sacrifices could make no one pure or holy before God. Instead, they all pointed toward the future High Priest and Sanctifier, Christ, and toward His body. He Himself became a holy sacrifice for us and has truly sanctified the Christian people—that is, His community or Church—through His own blood, so that its members, namely the true Christians, might become a holy, pure grain offering through Him and a sweet aroma of God in Him.

To this end, one might also draw upon what the Lord Jesus Christ says in the Gospel of John: "My food is to do the will of Him who sent me and to finish His work." This will and the completion of God's work, for which Christ was sent, was nothing other than that He should redeem us from the power of Satan, convert us from sins, renew our **old man** <note>A biblical term referring to the unregenerate, sinful nature of humanity before spiritual rebirth</note>, cleanse the conscience through His blood, sanctify us through His Spirit, and indeed prepare us within Himself as a pure grain offering well-pleasing to God. He was sent so that He might make us fit for all good works and cause us to be born again, to serve God the Lord in holiness and righteousness. Thus, the Lord Christ is not only the food and full sufficiency of His believers, but the believing members of His body (which is the Church) are also a "food"—that is, the delight, joy, and pleasure of Jesus Christ and the fullness of Him who fills all in all (Ephesians 1:23). This refers to the Lord Christ, from whom everything comes to us from God through faith at the beginning, leads us back to Him, and through Him reaches and rests in God.

Regarding this, the holy Ignatius also spoke, as Eusebius writes in the *Church Chronicle* <note>original: "Kirchen Chronica," referring to the *Ecclesiastical History* by Eusebius of Caesarea</note>: "I am a grain of Christ's wheat; I desire to be ground by the teeth of the wild beasts, so that I may become a pure bread or food for Christ."

It emphasizes that Christians serve as both the recipients of Christ's grace and a source of joy ("food") for Him, illustrated by the martyrdom of Ignatius.</summary>

# The 37th Chapter.

### That the Christian Faithful are also a Burnt Offering in Christ.

Just as the Christian faithful—who voluntarily surrender themselves to the Lord Christ and, through Him, offer themselves up to God the Father—become a spiritually pure **grain offering** <note>original: "Speißopfer"; referring to the meal or cereal offerings described in the Old Testament</note> through Christ, so too are they the spiritual, voluntary **burnt offering** in Christ. Following the pattern of the Lord Christ, they are prefigured by the burnt offerings of the Old Testament. For in the same way that the young bull brought for a burnt offering in the Old Testament was first slaughtered by the physical Mosaic priests, flayed of its skin, and hacked into pieces; and just as the head and the fat were placed upon the wood that lay on the altar (which stood before the Tabernacle of the Testimony and held both wood and fire):

> <note>The wood lies upon the altar in the fire; that is, the Cross lies upon Christ in His suffering.</note>

The entrails and the legs were washed with water, and everything was set ablaze on the altar as a burnt offering, to which the priest himself brought the fire, sprinkling the blood around the altar that stood before the Tabernacle of the Testimony.

In this same manner, our spiritual, heavenly High Priest, Jesus Christ, slaughters and puts to death the **old Adamite man** <note>A theological term for the fallen, sinful nature of humanity inherited from Adam</note> along with his beastly, evil desires. This is done through the living Word of God, which is sharper than any two-edged sword. His skin—that is, the old, stained coat of the flesh—is flayed from him; he is hacked to pieces—that is, displaced from his **selfhood** <note>original: "Eigenthum"; in this mystical context, it refers to the ego or the state of being possessed by one's own will rather than God's</note> and his old nature. The head and the fat, which signify the entire "old man," are crucified with Christ; his flesh, or the body of sin, is laid upon the wood of the Cross and set ablaze as a burnt offering in the fire of the Holy Spirit (which Christ the High Priest brings to it), so that a new man may emerge from him. This is then an offering that smells pleasant to the Lord.

> <note>Christ Himself must light the fire for the sacrifice that we are, if it is to be valid before God and smell pleasant.</note>

Likewise, the entrails and the thighs or feet—that is, man’s conscience, passions, and former conduct in the flesh...

<summary>This chapter provides a mystical interpretation of Old Testament sacrificial laws, explaining how the believer must be "slaughtered" and "burned" by the Holy Spirit to be reborn. It transforms the ritual of the burnt offering into a metaphor for the painful but necessary process of crucifying the sinful nature.</summary>

...is washed with the water of grace in Christ and is daily improved, of which the Lord also says: "He who is washed does not need to wash anything but his feet, for he is entirely clean." John 13:10. Thus, the High Priest Christ himself offers up his own blood, which was shed for the forgiveness of sins. With this blood, he daily sprinkles, cleanses, washes, and sanctifies the altar—that is, the heart of the faithful person—which is why the blood of Christ has been called a "blood of sprinkling" <note>A reference to the practice of sprinkling sacrificial blood on the altar in the Temple, now interpreted as a spiritual cleansing of the soul</note>. 1 Peter 1:2. This is also why the prophet David prays in Psalm 51:9, saying: "Purge me with hyssop <note>A small plant used in biblical times for ritual cleansing and sprinkling</note>, that I may be clean; wash me, that I may be whiter than snow." Here, the hyssop signifies the blood of Jesus Christ, the Son of God, which makes us clean from all sin. 1 John 1:7.

This now belongs to the true worship <note>original: "Gottesdienst"</note> of the New Testament. It was also prefigured through the ceremonial worship of the Old Testament, where the Mosaic physical priests slaughtered livestock with a physical knife, lit the offering, and sacrificed and burned it for physical purification to God. But the spiritual priest of the New Testament, Jesus Christ, slaughters the evil, beastly desires of the old sinful man with the spiritual knife or sword of the Word of God. He makes him new and alive through his Spirit, so that he may bring the Lord a pleasing grain offering <note>original: "Speißopfer"; the non-animal cereal or flour offering</note> in righteousness. Malachi 3:3.

However, that God the Lord was ultimately not concerned with figurative sacrifices, nor that true worship consisted of them or remains in ceremony, is also stated by the prophet Samuel <note>The German text identifies the speaker as "Daniel," but the biblical citation refers to the prophet Samuel speaking to King Saul in 1 Samuel 15:22</note> to King Saul, when Saul had kept the best of the plunder from the banned Amalekites to sacrifice to the Lord in Gilgal. He said: "Do you think that the Lord has delight in offerings and burnt offerings as much as in obeying the voice of the Lord? Behold, obedience is better than sacrifice, and to heed is better than the fat of rams." 1 Samuel 15:22. Likewise, Hosea 6:6: "I have pleasure in love and not in sacrifice, and in the knowledge of God and not in burnt offerings." Thus the prophet David also says clearly in Psalm 40:7: "Sacrifice and grain offering do not please you, but you have opened my ears. You want neither burnt offering nor sin offering; then I said: Behold, I come, in the book it is written of me. I delight to do your will, my God."

Micah 6:6–7: "With what shall I reconcile the Lord? With bowing before the high God? Shall I reconcile Him with burnt offerings and year-old calves? Do you think the Lord has pleasure in many thousands of rams?"

<summary>This page argues that Old Testament sacrificial rituals were mere symbols for the "true worship" of the New Testament: the internal cleansing of the heart by Christ and the replacement of animal sacrifice with spiritual obedience.</summary>

This is what Paul has beautifully explained in Hebrews 10:5–7, where instead of "you have opened my ears" <note>The author is noting a variation between the Hebrew text of Psalm 40:6 ("ears you have dug/opened") and the Greek/New Testament citation in Hebrews ("a body you have prepared").</note> he writes, *but a body you have prepared for me*, etc. *Burnt offerings and sin offerings did not please you; then I said: Behold, I come, in the volume of the book it is written primarily of me*, says the Lord. Above, when he had said, *Sacrifices and gifts, burnt offerings and sin offerings you did not want, nor did they please you*, which are offered according to the Law. Then he said: *Behold, I come to do your will, O God.* Here he abolishes the first (that is, the ceremonial worship with the sacrifices, priesthood, and temples, etc., of the Old Testament) so that he may establish the second (that is, the true, righteous worship of the New Testament, which stands in the spirit and in the truth of the living temple of God).

Behold, this was the counsel, purpose, decree, and final will of God: that Christ, through his coming, suffering, death, and through his self-sacrifice, should accomplish all our salvation. He was to fulfill all the figures <note>In historical theology, "figures" or "types" are Old Testament events or rituals that are seen as prophetic symbols of Christ.</note>, redeem us, bring forth the true truth, and gloriously establish a new holy worship with temples and sacrifices, as well as a new, holy, heavenly people and other things, after his resurrection through the Holy Spirit. Such a will of God was perfectly fulfilled by the Lord Christ, in which will we are sanctified once and for all through the sacrifice of the body of Jesus Christ. To him be praise, honor, and glory with God his Father, now and forever, Amen.

This I wanted to show from the prophet Malachi regarding the pure grain offering <term>grain offering: original "Speißopfer," a sacrifice consisting of flour and oil, which the author interprets here as the spiritual offering of a devoted Christian life</term>, which belongs to the Christian life and true worship of the New Testament, as it was also prefigured through the daily grain offering in the Old Testament. Following this, the prophecy of Daniel regarding the downfall or cessation of the daily sacrifice and worship, and concerning the "abomination of desolation" in the holy place—which the Lord Christ also mentions in the Gospel—is also well-suited for our consideration. However, having explained true worship, we will first say something about the servants of God, as follows.

# Chapter 38.

### Concerning the True Servants of God.

Holy Scripture calls those who serve God the Almighty according to His command and will "servants" or "ministers" of God. These are of two kinds, as I will distinguish hereafter. However, we wish to speak here of those who serve God the Lord in the aforementioned true and blessed worship <note>The German *Gottesdienst* literally means "God-service," referring here to the spiritual life and communal worship of the believer.</note> of the New Eternal Testament in holiness and righteousness. These are Christian believers, called and newly-reborn people, in whom God works and dwells, in whom He demonstrates His divine power through Christ for their own edification and the edification of others in faith, and who possess a good, joyful conscience in the service of God; wherein they also cooperate and serve God in His works.

Thus Saint Paul boasts in 2 Corinthians 1:12 that he has served God with a good conscience, and has conducted himself in the world in simplicity and divine sincerity, not in fleshly wisdom but in the grace of God. And in Hebrews 13:18, he writes: *We trust that we have a good conscience in all things*; by which Paul intends to show nothing else than that he knows and is assured by the Holy Spirit that Christ, with His power of grace <term>Gnadenkraft; the active, enabling power of God's grace working within the human soul</term>, works and dwells within him, through whom he serves God pleasingly, and in whom he is capable of all things. Indeed, that henceforth not Paul, but Christ lives in him; that it is not he, but the grace of God in and with him that performs such great and holy works, as we have heard before from the verses in Galatians 2:20 and 1 Corinthians 15:10. Chapter 19, 4.

Thus, one is called a true servant of God who voluntarily surrenders and offers himself from the heart to God and the Lord Christ for His service, praise, and glory. This is done so that henceforth he lives not according to human lusts, but according to the will of God (1 Peter 4:2). Indeed, that he lives not for himself, but for Him who died and rose again for him (2 Corinthians 5:15), that is, for the Lord Christ; one who always keeps God before his eyes in all his deeds and omissions, fearing and loving Him. This status is not due to monks and priests alone, but is the duty of all Christians and the elect <note>In the theology of this period, "the elect" refers to those chosen by God for salvation and true faith.</note>. For this reason, all true Christians are called "the saints" or the "holy people of God" in Scripture: because they are sanctified and consecrated to the service and as servants of God by the Spirit of the Lord, and are set apart from other people by their new Christian life and spiritual conduct, so that they no longer [participate] with the wicked world in its disorderly, godless...

<summary>This page defines the "true servant of God" as a person who serves through internal faith and a clear conscience rather than external ritual, emphasizing that this calling applies to all believers, not just the clergy.</summary>

nature they walk; though they remain in the world and are therefore persecuted by the world, they are instead blameless and pure or simple, and children of God, irreproachable in the midst of a crooked and perverse generation, among whom they shine as lights in the world. Philippians 2:15.

From this, one may surely perceive what a precious and glorious thing it is to be a righteous servant of God, the highest almighty Lord. His mind and spirit are daily renewed and transformed by the Holy Spirit, so that he may ever more test what the will of God his Lord is—what is good in His eyes, what is pleasing, acceptable, and perfect to Him. Romans 12:2.

This, then, is what it means to serve God with a good conscience: namely, when we serve God and His Spirit dwelling within us, performing His works by means of His grace. This occurs and begins when we are God-fearing, lead a Christian life of repentance, and rightly seize and possess Jesus Christ through faith, so that He rules in us, driving our heart, spirit, and senses toward all good works and the fulfillment of God’s commandments and will through His Spirit; so that henceforth we walk not according to the flesh, but in a new essence <note>German: *Wesen*. In this context, it refers to the fundamental nature or "way of being" of a person transformed by the Spirit.</note> of the Spirit, and remain steadfast in Christ until the end.

This is called serving God with a good conscience; just as Paul in 1 Timothy 3:9 requires a good conscience from all servants of God, and desires that they hold the mystery of faith and godliness (which is the knowledge of Jesus Christ according to the Spirit), each according to the grace given, in a pure and good conscience. He also testifies to this for the Corinthians when he writes that they are made rich in Christ Jesus in all things—in faith, in word or speech, and in knowledge (1 Corinthians 1:5)—and indeed in all kinds of diligence and love (2 Corinthians 8:7). Saint Paul lists faith, speech, and knowledge, as well as diligence and love, all of which are appropriate and belong to a true servant of God. But where this is not present—where Christ and His Spirit are not in the heart—one cannot serve God the Lord in a blessed <term>seliglich; describing a state of being "saved" or "blessed," relating to the soul's eternal happiness</term> or pleasing way; indeed, it goes so far that Paul says: a person in whom Christ does not dwell is still unfit and rejected. 2 Corinthians 13:5–7.

But as stated before, I speak here of the righteous servants of God, and of such a divine service <note>German: *Gottesdienst*. While often translated as "church service" or "worship," the author uses it here to mean the lifelong service or "liturgy" of a believer's daily actions.</note> that reaches into eternal life and concerns the salvation of the soul, occurring in spirit and in truth, which alone the Lord has promised to reward with the kingdom of heaven. Otherwise, God the Almighty is a great and powerful Lord, who has many various kinds of servants...

It distinguishes this internal, saving service from mere external or unfit religious practice.</summary>

and services, for when He commands, all creatures—even the evil spirit himself—must be obedient and serve His works. In this way, every power or authority is a servant of God, an avenger to bring punishment upon those who do evil (Romans 13 <note>Original: *Röm. 13*. The author refers to the biblical concept that even secular rulers are instruments of God’s will.</note>). ### Distinguishing a Servant of God and a Servant of Christ from an Enemy of the Holy Spirit.

We are not speaking here of such servants and service to God which occur externally to God and Christ—namely, in the natural order of earthly things, whether for divine punishment or human justice, without the rebirth of the heart. Rather, we speak of that grace-filled, blessed, and comforting service of God which occurs through the Holy Spirit in and out of God. In this service, there is nevertheless only one single servant and minister who has served God perfectly without any offense and pleased Him above all things, just as He earned eternal salvation for us through His service: namely, the man Jesus Christ our Lord. As it stands written of Him in the Prophets: *Behold, this is my servant, I uphold him; and my chosen, in whom my soul delights,* etc. (Isaiah 42:1 and 53:1). *Behold, my servant shall rule wisely, and shall be exalted and very high,* etc. (Ezekiel 34:11–16). *And I will help my flock,* etc. *I will raise up for them a shepherd of their own to tend them, namely my servant David; he shall tend them and be their shepherd. And I, the Lord, will be their God. But my servant David shall be the prince among them; I, the Lord, have spoken.* This was truly prophesied regarding the promised Messiah, Christ our Lord, and has now been blessedly fulfilled.

# Chapter 39.

### That the True Servants of God Serve God Rightly and Blessedly Only Through Christ and In Christ.

But regarding such a faithful, well-deserving servant and pleasing minister of God, our Lord Jesus Christ, who has perfectly fulfilled the will of His Father, and now no longer serves as He did in the days of His flesh, but with God

It presents Jesus Christ as the only perfect servant of God, fulfilling Old Testament prophecies of the "Servant of the Lord."</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 81 regarding Christ as the primary and perfect Servant of God. It explores how human "servants of God" derive their authority, ability, and forgiveness through their union with Christ’s perfect service.</meta>

rules and reigns with His Father, all other servants <note>German: *Knechte*. In this context, it refers to bondservants or those in total service to a master, contrasting with the "Son."</note> and true ministers of God must blessedly enjoy in God the Lord. Just as they must all take or draw from His fullness for true divine service—grace for grace—and whatever or wherever they have done too much or too little in their service, or still do, they must improve themselves solely through Him. They must call upon His <term>merit and satisfaction</term> <note>Theological terms referring to Christ’s payment of the debt of sin and His fulfillment of God’s law on behalf of humanity.</note> and recover through it. To Him, as the Monarch, King, and Lord, all servants of God should rightly be subject. *My servant David shall be the prince among them*, says God the Lord (as previously heard), who also blesses, initiates, and sanctifies them all for the service of God (2 Peter 1:21; Hebrews 2:11). Just as the entire service of God is fundamentally directed toward and established in Christ within God.

Therefore, whatever anyone lacks in the fulfillment of the service and will of God, or has ever lacked since youth, he may even today bring in and replace from the fullness of Christ, despite all his defects, neglect, and deficiency. Every believer can draw from that fullness if he holds to the fulfillment and satisfaction of Christ with a true, living faith, and makes himself a partaker of His merit through faith, comforting himself in the grace of God. From now on, however, he walks according to the will of God, so that he obediently surrenders his mouth, tongue, limbs, *etc.* to the service of Christ. Whoever is so minded and comes to Christ in this way—with true faith, with hatred of sin, with the purpose and beginning, as well as the progress, of a new Christian life—and who, in the grace of Christ, exerts all his strength to live rightly and godly, he becomes a true servant of God and a servant of righteousness in and through Christ. He will finally receive a righteous man’s reward: namely, the crown of eternal salvation.

For although the Lord Christ Himself primarily and fundamentally works and performs all good works in the servants of God through His Spirit, He is nevertheless so faithful, mild, and kind that He attributes to the servant that which is His own, on account of his faith, service, and obedience, or even his <term>cooperation</term> <note>Original: *Mitwirkens*. This refers to the theological concept of "synergism," where the believer's will responds to and works alongside divine grace, though the grace is the primary mover.</note>. Indeed, He so loves and honors His servants that He dwells in them and is with them, even working through them Himself (as heard before); and yet He graciously assigns such work to the servant and rewards it. However, the right servant well knows how to return this to Christ his Lord with all humility and gratitude, and knows how to point all good things—including his service to God—away from himself and toward the Master.

For thus we should act in the service of God and otherwise in all our doing, so that all honor and glory may be given purely and alone to Christ with God His Father, and that also

<summary>This page explains that believers can only serve God by drawing from the "fullness" and merit of Christ, who compensates for human failings. Of this, the Lord speaks in the Gospel of John to the Canaanite woman <note>The author attributes these words from John 4 to the Canaanite woman, though biblical tradition usually identifies the recipient of the "living water" discourse as the Samaritan woman at the well.</note>, where He says: "Whoever drinks of this water (He is speaking of physical water) will thirst again; but whoever drinks of the water that I shall give him (namely, the water of grace) will never thirst eternally; but the water that I shall give him will become in him a fountain of water springing up into eternal life." And thereafter in Chapter 7:37-38, the Lord cries out and says: "If anyone thirsts, let him come to me and drink; he who believes in me, as the Scripture says, out of his heart shall flow rivers of living water." But He said this of the Spirit, whom those who believed in Him were to receive; for the Spirit was not yet there (understand this as His manifest office of sanctification), because Jesus was not yet glorified.

It is also prophesied how the aforementioned daily pure grain offering, as well as the burnt offering and the right divine service, which consists in spirit and in truth, shall be desolated and abolished again by the Antichrist. In its place, a self-seeking divine service or human religion shall be established, in which many people would fall away from the faith and from the living God and His word, placing their trust, hope, and salvation in external, earthly things—without repentance, without the sacrifice of themselves, and without the true knowledge...

# Chapter 41.

### By which the antichristian kingdom with its members, false teachers, and servants may be recognized.

Concerning such a final, future abomination of the Antichrist, many warnings have been proclaimed here and there in the Gospel. He shall suppress the right knowledge of Him and, instead of the sound, well-ordered teaching of Christ and His Apostles, introduce a mixed, erroneous doctrine leading to fleshly security and godlessness, with false comfort and hope for the forgiveness of sins, thereby disrupting the conversion of sinners and true <term>repentance</term> <note>original: "Buße"; implies both the internal change of heart and the outward act of penance.</note>. He will present himself in worldly splendor and glitter so that he almost wishes to be accepted and regarded as Christ the Savior, Redeemer, and Forgiver of sins himself (against which deception the Lord Christ faithfully warned and commanded us to be on guard, Matth. 24:24-26). For this reason, he is called the Opponent or Antichrist in Holy Scripture, as he is fundamentally an enemy of the crucified and glorified Lord Christ and of all true godliness. Just as he directs all that is his against Christ and against His kingdom, he nevertheless wishes to be regarded as a faithful friend, caretaker, or steward—indeed, a preacher and teacher of grace and of Christ's kingdom—as if he were promoting nothing but the <term>affairs of Christ</term> <note>original: "Händel Christi"; refers to the business, dealings, or historical events associated with Christ's ministry.</note> at Jerusalem and the temple of God. # Chapter 42.

### Concerning the final, solemn times of the Church and the general downfall of Christian life.

This now is the mystery of the vision of the Prophet Daniel: how in the last days—with the cessation of the daily pure <term>sacrifice</term> <note>original: "Speißopfer"; referring to the grain or meal offering. In this context, it symbolizes the loss of pure, sincere worship.</note>, the desolation of true divine service, and the downfall of the proper Christian life, as well as the soul-saving knowledge of Jesus Christ—things will become dangerous and dire within the Christian Church. The Lord will be to us like a spring that will no longer flow, Jeremiah 15:18. Even beautiful virgins and youths shall then faint from thirst, Amos 8:13. Likewise, at such a time there will be a great hunger to hear the Word of God (that is, the sound, pure, and healing doctrine of Christ and His knowledge). They will run to and fro to seek the word of the Lord and yet not find it. The sun will go down at noon, and the land will become dark in broad daylight. In short, it is a day of tumult and destruction and confusion in the "Valley of Vision" from the Lord, the Lord of Hosts, etc., Isaiah 22:1. All this is for the sake of the people's iniquity, which God will remember, visiting their sins upon them. The prophet Hosea also prophesies of this when he says: "For there is no faithfulness, no love, and no word of God in the land; rather, blasphemy, lying, murder, stealing, and adultery have gained the upper hand, and one blood-guilt follows another; therefore the land shall stand in misery, and it shall go ill for all inhabitants," Hosea 4:3. The fig tree will then not blossom, and there will be no fruit on the vines, <note>The source cites Habakkuk 4:17, though the biblical book of Habakkuk traditionally only contains three chapters; this likely refers to the famous passage in Habakkuk 3:17.</note> Habakkuk 4:17. Furthermore, the Lamentations of Jeremiah—concerning the wretched desolation of the City of God and the robbery of its sanctuary, its ornaments, and its treasures, as well as the downfall and destruction of its people—is entirely directed toward this.

Concerning such destruction and desolation of Christianity according to the thousandfold-cunning [intentions] of the evil spirit (who is the head of the Antichrist)...

# Chapter 43.

## Concerning the Reformation of the Church, and the Restoration of True Worship through the Spirit of the Lord.

Regarding such comfort in the revelation of the Antichrist and the further building and reformation of the church, and the re-establishment of true worship before the Last Day through the Spirit of the Lord, we have many beautiful prophecies in <term>Isaiah</term>, <term>Jeremiah</term>, and also in <term>Daniel</term> and the other prophets, as well as in the <term>Revelation of John</term>; namely, that all the aforementioned destruction and seduction shall only last for a time. Then the sanctuary will be consecrated once again, for it has been determined how long it shall last, at which point, after the completion of the time of tribulation, the great prince <term>Michael</term> <note>original: "Michael"; interpreted by the author as a figure for Christ himself</note> (that is, the Lord Christ) will arise and rescue His people.

And you, Daniel, hide these words, says the Lord, and seal this writing until the final time; then many shall come across it and find great understanding, etc. Isaiah 29, Revelation 5. And many shall be cleansed, purified, and tested. And the ungodly shall lead an ungodly life, and all the ungodly shall not heed it; but those who are wise will take it to heart, Daniel 12, and Hosea 3:5. At that time, the children of Israel shall be converted and seek the Lord their God and their King David, and they shall honor the Lord and His grace in the final time.

This also agrees with what is written in Isaiah 11, where he says: The Lord will at that time once more stretch out His hand to recover the remnant of His people who are left from Assyria, Egypt, Pathros, Ethiopia <note>original: "Mohrenland"; historically used to refer to the lands of Africa, particularly Cush or Ethiopia in a biblical context.</note>, etc. And He will raise a banner for the nations and bring together the outcasts of Israel and gather the scattered of Judah from the four corners of the earth. Shortly before this, it is written: He will strike the earth with the rod of His mouth, and with the breath of His lips He will slay the ungodly.

Jeremiah also prophesied of this almost throughout the 30th chapter, where among other things it is written: I will restore you to health and heal your wounds, says the Lord, because they called you the outcast, saying, "This is Zion, for whom no one cares." Thus says the Lord: Behold, I will restore the fortunes of the tents of Jacob and have mercy on his dwellings; the city shall be rebuilt from its ashes, and the temple shall stand as it ought to stand, and from there shall proceed songs of praise and the sound of joy. And shortly before that: and they shall serve the Lord their God and their King David, whom I will raise up for them, Jeremiah 30 <note>The "King David" mentioned here is interpreted by the author as a reference to the Messiah, Jesus Christ</note>. It is also stated there, just as in Daniel, that such prophecy shall happen and be experienced in the <term>latter days</term> <note>The final period of human history before the Last Judgment</note>. Regarding this, see further the 31st chapter of Jeremiah, what the Lord has also graciously promised there.

And Zechariah 8: Thus says the Lord: I have returned to Zion and will dwell in Jerusalem, so that Jerusalem shall be called a City of Truth, and the mountain of the Lord of Hosts a Mountain of Holiness, etc. If it seems impossible in the eyes of the remnant of this people in these times, should it therefore also be impossible in my eyes? says the Lord of Hosts.

Now, although such and similar prophecies are to be understood beyond the historical sense of the letter—primarily concerning the time of Christ and the office of His redemption; also, concerning the outpouring of the Holy Spirit and the first Apostolic church assembly and edification; and subsequently concerning His kingdom, the resurrection of the flesh, the final judgment, eternal life, or the state of all the elect—we nevertheless do not wish to reject the idea (namely, because the prophets are mindful of the final time) that it may also be understood as the reformation and rebuilding or the gathering of the miserable, scattered churches devastated by Satan and the <term>Antichrist</term> <note>original: "Wiederchrist"</note>, which is to occur before the Last Day.

But this is not to be understood as the <term>Chiliasts</term> <note>Followers of Chiliasm (Millenarianism), who believe in a literal 1,000-year earthly reign of Christ</note> do, who, along with the Jews, hope for a future for the Messiah and a physical kingdom of a thousand years on earth.

<summary>The author cites Old Testament prophecies from Isaiah, Jeremiah, and Zechariah to argue that the Church will undergo a spiritual reformation and restoration before the Last Judgment. He distinguishes this "latter day" renewal from the literal 1,000-year earthly kingdom predicted by Millenarians.</summary>

Nor is it meant in such a way as if a gathered Church is to be expected here whose members were so completely perfect, so entirely pure, without any sin or blemish of the flesh, that they no longer needed Christ and His grace; indeed, that they would not need to pray in the <term>Lord’s Prayer</term> <note>original: "Vater Unser"; the central Christian prayer taught by Jesus</note>, "forgive us our debts." Much less do we mean such a Reformation as if all godlessness were to be uprooted before the <term>Last Day</term> <note>The final day of judgment at the end of the world</note>, and that righteousness alone should dwell in this perishable world.

Such is not at all our meaning regarding the Reformation or the rebuilding of the Church. Rather, it is thus: that we hope the Lord and Head of the Church, Jesus Christ, will (as He has already graciously begun) spread His heavenly kingdom with more power and blessing over us poor ones, gather the scattered children of God in the Holy Spirit, yes, beget many through the Word of Truth, and let His knowledge and glory break through more brightly; that He will endow the Church and its members with more grace, light, understanding, and richer spiritual gifts. That people will also be more zealous, more spiritual, more God-fearing, more pious, and more godly than is sadly seen today—where one sows much and brings in little, where everyone is zealous for his own house while the Lord’s house lies so desolate <note>This is a reference to the prophet Haggai 1:4–9, where the people are criticized for building their own fine homes while neglecting the temple</note>; indeed, it stands like an oak with dry leaves and like a garden without water, since no one wants to truly lament the Church's damage or properly concern themselves with her plague. But the God of Israel will have mercy on her; He can yet open rivers of water on the heights and springs in the midst of the fields. Isaiah 41:18.

Briefly, this is our prayer and comforting hope toward God: that the Lord Jesus Christ, in the current <term>apostasy</term> <note>The abandonment or renunciation of religious faith</note>, division, and scattering of the churches, will yet graciously bring forth the only true means (which stands in His salvific knowledge and in the revelation of His kingdom and spiritual truth) for a Christian <term>Concordia</term> <note>original: "Concordia"; a Latin term for "harmony" or "agreement," often used to describe the unity of belief within the Church</note> for all the elect. We hope He will yet send out many pious, faithful workers into His harvest before the Last Day, who seek only the glory of God and the salvation of men—who preach the Gospel of grace and the sound doctrine of Christ and His Apostles, not mixed with the leaven of human ordinances, nor with philosophy, much less with fleshly affections. These workers will also teach the Gospel not according to the letter alone in external words—without the internal growth and fruit of the people—but purely and clearly, in the power of God and in the Holy Spirit, with much divine blessing and prosperity according to <term>Apostolic simplicity</term> <note>A return to the humble, straightforward practice of faith seen in the earliest Christian communities</note>. Through their service, the Lord will reform, unite, and convert the hearts of the elect through His grace. And just as errors should be rightly set aside in the light of grace, so one should also strive against sin.

Just as little should we delay our repentance and the improvement of our lives because of such hope; rather, we should all the more and all the sooner cleanse ourselves from sins in the blood of Christ, hate those sins, and lead a Christian, godly life today—not tomorrow, as the prophet admonishes—in the preceding grace and fear of God. We should seek the kingdom of God and His righteousness, and wait daily for the coming of the Lord for judgment (to which all the Apostles point).

I have chosen to recount this at greater length because some accuse me of despising current Christian assemblies and the gifts of God in favor of a hope for a brighter enlightenment of Christ and His spiritual riches; or as if I wished to separate myself from the Holy Apostolic Christian Church and commanded others to wait for a completely perfect future Church, or wished to insist upon it (and I know not what else they devise). This should also be rightfully fulfilled in the rebuilding or reformation of the new spiritual Temple—that is, the Christian Church, in which God dwells, and in which a Christian life and the right worship in Spirit and in Truth is maintained—just as all other figures must be fulfilled.

# Chapter 44.

### Testimony of the Glory of Christ and the future Reformation of the Church through Christ, from the Book of Revelation. <note>original: "Offenbarung Johannis"</note>

However, after the mournful, miserable time of the Church, and after various deceptions, dispersions, and persecutions by the Antichrist <note>The "Widerchrist" or Antichrist was viewed by many Reformation-era writers not just as a single figure, but as a period of spiritual corruption within the institutional church.</note>, a more gracious visitation and a new light—with a gentler distribution of the gifts of the Holy Spirit from God through Christ—is to be hoped for. This is clearly proven in many places by the Revelation of John. (For that book described the state of the Church from that time until very near the end of the world.) In this, all other prophets also harmonize in a straightforward and lovely way.

Furthermore, it presents beautiful testimonies to all Christians regarding the glory of Jesus Christ, the divine essence, and the characteristics of his glorified humanity. From these, it is also irrefutably proven that the man Jesus Christ is the true Son of God (who is called there the "slain Lamb"), to be called upon, worshipped, and honored with God in one divine essence, in one heavenly glory, and in the same power, might, radiance <note>original: "Klarheit," referring to the divine brightness or "transfigured" quality of Christ.</note>, dignity, and honor. He is also an heir and distributor of all divine riches: for it was the good pleasure of God the Father that in him all fullness should dwell, so that all other people, through faith, might take from his fullness, grace for grace. Colossians 1:19 <note>original: "Coloss. 1, 19"</note>; John 1:16 <note>original: "Joh. 1, 16"</note>.

Since it also belongs to true worship—indeed, it is the most prominent form of worship—that we rightly recognize Christ and God his Father, and honor him rightly with faith and adoration, and since the knowledge of Christ and his benefits is essential above all things for the Reformation of the Church, we wish first to recount several testimonies of the glory of the man Christ from the aforementioned prophetic book of the Revelation of John.

First, it is undeniable among all right-thinking theologians that by the slain or slaughtered Lamb, the crucified Son of God, Jesus of Nazareth, is understood. Indeed, he is the very man of whom it is written in Revelation 1 <note>original: "Apoc. 1."</note>, who has eyes like flames of fire, from whose mouth goes a sharp two-edged sword, whose face shines like the bright sun, who holds the seven stars in his hand, etc. And after that...

# Chapter 44.

### Testimony of the Glory of Christ and the Future Reformation of the Church through Christ, from the Book of Revelation.

However, that after the mournful and wretched time of the Church, and after various deceptions, dispersals, and persecutions by the Antichrist <note>The author uses "Widerchrist" to refer to an individual or power that opposes Christ, a common theme in Reformation-era apocalyptic writing.</note>, a grace-filled visitation and a new light—with a more gentle distribution of the gifts of the Holy Spirit from God through Christ—is to be hoped for, is clearly proven in many places by the Revelation of John. (Just as that book described the state of the Church from that time until very near the end of the world.) In this, all other prophets harmonize beautifully and directly.

Moreover, it presents beautiful testimonies to all Christians regarding the glory of Jesus Christ, the divine essence, and the attributes of his glorified humanity. From these, it is irrefutably proven that the man Jesus Christ is the true Son of God (who is there called the slain Lamb), to be called upon, worshipped, and honored with God in one divine essence, in one heavenly glory, in the same power, strength, clarity, dignity, and honor; and that he is also an heir and distributor of all divine riches. For it was the pleasure of God the Father that in him all fullness should dwell, so that all other people might take from his fullness through faith, grace upon grace. Colossians 1:19; John 1:16.

Since it also belongs to true worship—indeed, it is the most distinguished form of worship—that we rightly recognize Christ and God his Father, and honor him correctly with faith and adoration; and just as the knowledge of Christ and his benefits must, above all things, belong to the <term>reformation</term> <note>German: *Reformirung*. In this context, it refers to the spiritual renewal and cleansing of the church.</note> of the Church, we wish first to recount several testimonies of the glory of the man Christ from the aforementioned prophetic book of the Revelation of John.

First, it is undeniable among all <term>orthodox</term> <note>original: "rechtsinnigen," meaning those who hold "right" or "straight" opinions, referring to traditional Protestant theologians.</note> theologians that by the killed or slaughtered Lamb mentioned there, the crucified Son of God, Jesus of Nazareth, is understood—indeed, that very man of whom it is written in Revelation 1, who has eyes like flames of fire, from whose mouth a sharp two-edged sword goes out, whose face shines like the bright sun, who has the seven stars in his hand, etc. And after that...

<meta>This page continues the theological argument from Chapter 44, providing specific scriptural proofs from the Book of Revelation (the Apocalypse) to demonstrate the divine status of Christ’s humanity and his role in the spiritual renewal of the Church.</meta>

... it further follows that the mentioned man is the Son of God, that he has received all authority from the Father, that he also sits with God the Father on his throne, and is the one who searches the kidneys and hearts <note>This phrase refers to the biblical idiom for a person’s innermost thoughts and emotions, often translated as "mind and heart."</note>, and will give to each according to their works. Thus it is quickly understood from this that, although God and man are two natures, there is no essential difference between them in the person of Christ in terms of power, honor, riches, glory, and capacity; rather, God and man are one Son of God and one Christ, to whom all divine honor is due according to his humanity as well as according to his divinity. As it is written there concerning the slain Lamb—without whose Spirit the Christian Church can nevermore be reformed—therefore, one must pray with all earnestness and diligence for this Spirit and for the knowledge of Christ.

Secondly, the divine glory of the crucified man Jesus Christ our Lord is proven by the fact that only the slaughtered Lamb, the Lion of the tribe of Judah, the Root of David, was worthy to take the sealed book of the mysteries of God from the right hand of him who sat upon the throne, and to open its seven seals—indeed, to send the Spirit of God into all lands—which is surely a certain indication of the complete divinization <note>original: "Gottwerdung." This term describes the process of Christ's human nature being fully united with and glorified by his divine nature.</note>, equality, and glory of the man (that is, the Lamb) with God in the one person of Christ.

Thirdly, since John saw that the mentioned Lamb had seven horns and seven eyes—which, as John himself interprets, are the spirits of God sent into all lands—it is clearly indicated thereby that the man Jesus Christ, as well as God, has received from God his Father the Holy Spirit with his seven—indeed, many manifold—gifts, to pour out over all flesh, through whom he also rules and governs in the Christian Church, which is his body, as the head. Acts 2:17–18.

Fourthly, that there also, in Revelation 5:8–10, the four living creatures and the twenty-four elders testify to the glory of the man Christ, as they fall down before the Lamb, worship the man Christ, play for him with their heavenly harps, and sing a new song with praise and honor for his redemption as the renewer of all things. And thereafter in the seventh chapter, where John again saw a great multitude that no one could number, from all nations and peoples and tongues, who all cried out with loud voices: Salvation belongs to Him who sits on the throne, our God, and to the Lamb.

Fifthly, the divine glory of the Lamb, and the equality of Christ’s two natures <note>The "two natures" refers to the traditional Christian doctrine that Jesus is simultaneously fully divine and fully human.</note> in glory, is also proven by this: that in the blood of the Lamb, the sins of all Christian believers are washed away, and their clothes—that is, their consciences—are made white and prepared for the service of God. This Lamb, who is in the midst of God’s throne, also feeds them and leads them to the living fountain of water. How should the Lamb, namely the man Christ, not also according to His human nature be, do, and be capable of everything that God is? Therefore, God and the Lamb are often joined together in one power, might, dignity, honor, and reality, and are finally concluded as one with God. This is to signify that Christ, according to both His natures in glory, is one God and Lord with God the Father and the Holy Spirit.

Sixthly, we also have a glorious testimony of this in the twelfth chapter, when the seventh angel sounds his trumpet, and there were great voices in heaven saying: "The kingdoms of the world have become the kingdoms of our Lord and of His Christ, and He shall reign from eternity to eternity." By this, the divine glory of Christ, the Son of God the Father, is once again signified.

It is also written in Chapter 17, Verse 14, that the Lamb—that is, the man Christ—is a Lord of all lords and a King of all kings. However, since all Christians know that there is only one Lord and King in the kingdom of heaven, it must follow indisputably that the power, the reign, and the unity of God's essence is common to God and the man Christ in the person of the Lord. Yes, that the man Jesus Christ, in and with God, is our Lord and God.

As it then also stands hereafter in the nineteenth chapter, that Almighty God has taken up the kingdom. But who is He who has taken up the kingdom? None other than the Lamb and the man Jesus Christ; thus it follows that the Lamb Christ, in his un-extinguished human nature <note>original: "unabgetilgter menschlicher Natur." The author emphasizes that Christ does not lose his humanity even when he is fully glorified as God.</note>, is nothing other than God, and has no other essence than the glorious eternal divine essence, which He possesses by inheritance and eternally.

Ninthly, the aforementioned equality and unity of the glories of God and the Lamb are proven even more clearly when the angel shows John the New Jerusalem, the holy city of God, whose streets were pure gold. Concerning this, John writes: "And I saw no temple therein, for the Lord, Almighty God, is its temple and the Lamb. And the city needed no sun, nor the moon, to shine in it, for the glory (or clarity <note>The German word *Klarheit* suggests a divine radiance or brightness that makes things visible and pure.</note>) of God enlightened it; and its lamp is the Lamb, and the nations that are saved walk in that same light."

There we have it clearly stated that the Lamb is the lamp of the city of God—that is, the Christian Church and all its members. Therefore, the Lamb, Christ, even according to His human nature as well as His divine nature, is full of the brightly shining light and radiance of the entire Godhead; indeed, He is everything that God is and is capable of. Thus, the humanity of Christ—His body, flesh, and blood—remains in God with such immeasurable clarity, unconsumed and unextinguished, and also unmixed <note>This refers to the "unmixed" nature of Christ's two identities: He is fully God and fully man without the two being blurred into a third thing.</note> with the Godhead. But who can understand or recognize such a thing? Only living, true faith can, because it is too high and incomprehensible for all human reason. This is also why human reason seeks all kinds of glosses <note>interpretive excuses or clever wordplay</note> to pull the Scripture toward its own fleshly understanding—Scripture which testifies to such immeasurable glory of Christ's flesh—because reason can in no way grasp the verses as they simply sound.

Since Saint John now writes that not only Almighty God, but also the Lamb is the temple, as well as the lamp and the light of the city of God: it follows irrefutably that the humanity and divinity of Christ are one glory, one essence, and divine clarity. Indeed, it follows that the Son of God, Christ, together with His Father, is our light, our life, our Lord and God, even according to His humanity. As it also follows later concerning the Christian believers: They shall need no light, for the Lord (that is, Christ) shall enlighten them.

Tenthly, the divine glory of the body, flesh, and blood of Christ is proven by the fact that the angel shows John a pure stream of water, clear as crystal, proceeding from the throne of God and of the Lamb into the middle of the streets of the city of God. These streams of water signify nothing other than the Holy Spirit, and show that the Holy Spirit proceeds not only from God, but also from the Lamb—that is, from the man and body of Jesus Christ—over all citizens of the city of God into the hearts and souls of all true believers. How then could the man Jesus Christ not be, in clarity, honor, power, kingdom, and ability, everything that God is?

See how the angel previously said the Lamb would enlighten them. Here he says, God the Lord will enlighten them. What he previously said of the Lamb, he says here of God the Lord.

I wanted to introduce this testimony here, in the chapter on the **reformation of the churches** and the **restoration of true worship**, so that one might first learn to rightly recognize the Head and the Foundation of the church—Christ in His glory—as well as His community, the Christian church, according to the testimony of Holy Scripture, before considering or acting upon anything useful regarding church reformation or reconstruction. ...hope. I wish to recount these testimonies from the text with a short instruction and leave them to the Christian reader to ponder further.

First, the falling away <note>original: *Abfall*, often translated as apostasy; a central theme in Reformation-era critiques of the established church</note> of the universal apostolic Christian church is to be noted early on in the second and third chapters of that book.

There the Spirit commands the angel of the congregation at Laodicea, among other things, to write: "You say, I am rich and have become wealthy, and need nothing; and you do not know that you are wretched and miserably poor, blind and naked; I counsel you," says Christ the Lord, "that you buy gold from me that is refined by fire, so that you may become rich, and that you put on white garments, so that the shame of your nakedness may not be revealed; and anoint your eyes with eye salve, that you may see." Revelation 3.

With this, the Lord Christ in the Holy Spirit revokes everything that has fallen away from him toward external things—trying to find salvation in them—or is otherwise blameworthy, and faithfully exhorts us to his true, soul-saving knowledge and the reception of his benefit and grace.

### To pray to Christ for his grace and living Word; the letter alone creates only a delusion and a vain boasting of divine wealth.

Regarding the falling away, discord, and destruction of the church in the last days, we also read in the twelfth chapter how the great red dragon, Satan and Antichrist (who always acts against Christ and his truth) with his tail (which signifies power, force, and worldly authority) draws down a third of the stars (by which the <term>prelates</term> <note>high-ranking church officials</note>, scholars, and preachers or leaders of the church are meant) from heaven (that is, from the contemplation of heavenly things and from Christ) and throws them to the earth. That is to say, he seduces them and lures them to himself with great possessions, money, worldly honor, splendor, success, pleasure, and glory, so that they become earthly-minded and forget Christ and his divine service. Thus, some even deny the Lord who bought them; indeed, they will love and seek the honor of men more than the honor of God and Christ. All of this, according to the evidence of Scripture, belongs to the kingdom of the Antichrist who is to come in the last days. 2 Peter 2.

...of the churches and the redemption of the faithful through the coming or through the appearance of the Spirit of God is signified.

Thus, the vision of the two witnesses, whom God promises to send in the final days of his church, signifies nothing other than the Reformation and rebuilding of the desolate, poor, and troubled church of Christ. In this way, the Church Fathers Tertullian, Ambrose, Augustine, and others have interpreted this as the coming of Enoch and Elijah <note>In traditional Christian eschatology, Enoch and Elijah were often identified as the "two witnesses" from Revelation 11 because they were taken to heaven without dying in the Old Testament and were expected to return to oppose the Antichrist.</note>, who, in the midst of all error, scattering, and deception, shall position themselves against the Antichristian kingdom with the power of God. They shall write, preach, and teach against it—indeed, they shall bring forth the pure divine truth again. This shall also serve the Lord Christ so that he will reform his scattered, wretched church—devastated by the Antichrist—through the Holy Spirit. He will restore true worship and a righteous Christian life, and rectify all that has been mixed and darkened under the kingdom of human tradition, as well as the [dead] letter with philosophical and sophisticated false teachings in the church. He will improve all that was devastated by various false worships, idolatry, and hypocrisy under the Antichrist, measuring the temple of God,*) and building, increasing, and gathering his community in the Holy Spirit; so that the church may then grow through the King of Grace, Jesus Christ, into the holy temple of God, and Christ may dwell steadfastly in the hearts of the faithful, shining through them in love and being rightly recognized in his magnificence and glory, and his power and kingdom may become known to all the world.

To such an office, which the Lord Christ will then conduct with even more power in the Holy Spirit, the two prophets Enoch and Elijah—and indeed many others—shall serve him before the end of the world. Through this, the consciences of men shall no longer be built upon sand, but shall be blessedly founded and built upon the foundation, rock, and cornerstone, Jesus Christ.

Likewise, the mystery or vision of the Revelation of the painted, unchaste woman in the scarlet coat <note>original: *rothen Rock*; this refers to the "Whore of Babylon" from Revelation 17.</note>, who sat upon a scarlet-colored beast full of names of blasphemy, having ten horns and seven heads, with whom the kings of the earth commit adultery and from whose great luxury her merchants grow rich, <term>etc.</term> <note>original: *2c.*, an abbreviation for *et cetera*.</note>, also belongs here for consideration; and that she has a golden cup in her hand full of abominations and the uncleanness of her fornication—that is, full of error, deception, and idolatry—and she has become drunk from the blood of the saints and the witnesses of Jesus Christ.
